<meta>This page follows page 93. While the previous page was illegible due to damage, this page appears to be a new title page for a specific work by Valentin Weigel, signaling a fresh section in the manuscript collection.</meta>

# D I A L O G U E
## on Christianity:
<note>original: "de Christianismo"</note>

->That is /<-

# A Christian /
## highly important and necessary colloquy or conversation between three of the most eminent persons in the world / namely, the Listener, the Preacher, and Death.
<note>The original Latin terms used are "Auditoris" (the one who hears), "Concionatoris" (the preacher or orator), and "Mortis" (Death personified).</note>

->How man is taught by God / reborn from God / physically, inwardly, and outwardly united with Christ / and becomes blessed and righteous / and not outside of Him /<-
<note>The phrase "physically... united" (original: "leibhafftig... vereyniget") refers to the mystical belief in a real, substantial union between the believer and the divine, rather than a purely symbolic one.</note>

->Composed by<-

# Master Valentin Weigel, former pastor at Zschopau.
<note>Valentin Weigel (1533–1588) was a German theologian and philosopher. He kept his most radical mystical views secret during his lifetime; his works were published posthumously and became very influential in radical Pietism.</note>

->Printed at Neustadt by Johann Knuber / in the year of Christ<-

### 1618.

<summary>This is the title page for a dialogue written by the mystic Valentin Weigel, structured as a conversation between a listener, a preacher, and Death regarding the nature of spiritual rebirth and union with Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Valentin Weigel, dialogue, spiritual rebirth, mystical union, 17th-century printing, Zschopau, Neustadt, personification of Death</keywords>

# Explanation of these three
## principal persons in
### the world.

So that you, favorable reader, may understand who is comprised within these persons, know that this <term>Dialogue <note>original: "Dialogus"</note></term> divides the whole world into three persons: namely, the Listener, the Preacher, and Death.

The <term>Listener <note>original: "Auditor"</note></term> is not only the layman or common man, but all those who allow themselves to be taught, guided, and led by the <term>Clergy <note>original: "Geistlichen Standt"</note></term> from the pulpit, or through scriptures, books, <term>commentaries <note>original: "Commenten"</note></term>, and teachings. This includes Emperors, Kings, Princes, Lords, Counts, Knights, Nobles, commoners, citizens, peasants, the learned and unlearned, young and old, woman and man, etc. All of these are encompassed and understood by the word <term>Unlearned <note>original: "Idiota." In this period, the term "Idiota" did not mean a "fool," but rather a layperson or someone without formal academic degrees in theology or Latin.</note></term>, <term>Listener <note>original: "Auditor"</note></term>, or "one who hears."

The <term>Preacher <note>original: "Concionator" or "Prædicant"</note></term> encompasses the Pope,

<meta>catchword: Cardinal/</meta>

<summary>This page defines the three central archetypes of the dialogue—the Listener, the Preacher, and Death—explaining that the "Listener" represents all people, regardless of social rank, who receive spiritual instruction.</summary>

<page-num>4</page-num>

<header>Preface.</header>

Cardinals, Bishops, Prelates, Doctors, high and low; all interpreters of Scripture in universities, consistories <note>A consistory is a formal council or court of religious leaders.</note>, foundations, monasteries, etc. Everything that teaches in <term>public <note>original: "publico"</note></term>, whether orally or in writing, and explains the Law of God. Also the lawyers, the whole <term>Chair of Moses and Peter <note>original: "Stuel Mosis vnd Petri." This refers to the traditional seats of religious and legal authority—Moses for the Law and Peter for the Church.</note></term>.

<term>Death <note>original: "Mors"</note></term> encompasses Jesus Christ, the one killed and crucified: his birth, flight, baptism, Last Supper, contempt, mockery, persecution, crucifixion, and burial. In summary, it encompasses everything that Christ spoke, suffered, performed, and taught. He [Death] is one with Christ, for without dying, Christ would not be Christ, and no one has ever become or remained a partaker of Christ without death. Therefore, he [Death] justly calls himself an ambassador of Him who calls Himself a King of all kings and a **LORD** of all **LORDS**.

And just as the <term>Preacher <note>original: "Concionator"</note></term>—that is, the Clerical Estate—rules from the Chair of Moses and Peter over all heads of state, potentates, and estates in the whole world; for everyone falls down before this estate and says:

> Our Father who art on earth, you have
> the power to loose and to bind, to close and to open
> Heaven. (All, I say) do this, those who have their conduct on
> earth—that is, in the "old man"—and not in Heaven,
> that is, in the new creature. <term>Philippians 3 <note>original: "Philip. 3." Refers to Philippians 3:20, contrasting earthly and heavenly citizenship.</note></term>. Whose

# Preface.

A iij

names not written in the <term>Book of Life <note>A biblical concept from the Book of Revelation referring to the record of those destined for salvation.</note></term> worship the beast, etc. Just as now (I say) the <term>Preacher <note>original: "Concionator"</note></term> rules over all people who dwell on earth, whose conduct is not in heaven—that is, who are not in Christ. So also <term>Death <note>original: "Mors"</note></term> is and remains the person sent by Christ, a **LORD** over all kings, emperors, princes, etc. And everything that lives under the sun cannot defend itself against Death. From his hands no cruel murderer nor strong warrior has escaped; he seizes them and strangles them all. Therefore, reader, pay attention to these three persons—what they encompass and what they say—for then you will experience and recognize with good discernment what is meant by:

->**Outside the Church there is no salvation.** <note>original: "Extra Ecclesiam nulla salus."</note><-
->**Outside faith there is no salvation.** <note>original: "Extra fidem nulla salus."</note><-
->**Outside love there is no salvation.** <note>original: "Extra dilectionem nulla salus."</note><-
->**Outside Christ there is no salvation.** <note>original: "Extra Christum nulla salus."</note><-

Read it and rejoice with me; praise God, here and in the world to come forever, Amen.

Since it has now been shown who is to be understood by these persons, it is also not useless to mention and briefly touch upon the **subject matter** or **arguments** <note>original: "Materiam oder argumenta"</note> which each person specifically addresses.

<margin>Listener.</margin>
As the <term>Common Man <note>original: "Idiota." In this context, it refers to a layperson or someone without formal academic training, rather than a lack of intelligence.</note></term> or **Listener**, through enlightenment

<summary>This page concludes the description of the Preacher and Death as figures of absolute authority over the worldly, while introducing the theological necessity of being "in Christ." It transitions into explaining the specific roles and arguments of the three figures, beginning with the enlightened Listener.</summary>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the introduction to the three figures mentioned on the previous page, specifically focusing on the first figure: the Listener (or "Auditor"). It elaborates on his spiritual understanding of the mystical union between the believer and Christ.</meta>

<page-num>6</page-num>

<header>Preface.</header>

[...the Listener has] understood so much from the Holy Spirit out of the Scriptures that a person must be bodily united with **GOD** through **Jesus Christ** as a new <term>Creature <note>The author uses this term to refer to the "New Man" or the transformed spiritual self born through faith.</note></term>, born of **GOD** himself; this is the ultimate purpose for which man was created and redeemed. Namely, that he dwells in God, and **GOD** in him—not only in the world to come, but also in this present time through faith in Christ—which he confirms with many testimonies from the Scriptures.

Secondly, that a person must be drawn by the Father if he wishes to come to the Son—that is, to walk and live in Christ. Without this anointing or enlightenment through the Spirit, all sermons and books are in vain; knowledge must flow from within, and it cannot be brought in from the outside without the inner testimony of the Holy Spirit.

The Listener also further confirms the essential indwelling of Christ from Divine Scripture and powerfully promotes the <term>mortification of the flesh <note>original: "mortificationem carnis"</note></term>, which is the <term>death of Christ in us <note>original: "mortem Christi in nobis"</note></term>, so that the inner, New Man may live, while the outer man is killed and crucified with Christ. For if we were not willing to die with Christ through true <term>repentance <note>original: "Busse"</note></term> in faith, then Christ would have died in vain, and Baptism and the <term>Holy Supper <note>original: "Heilige Nachtmahl"; a reference to the Eucharist or Communion.</note></term> would have been established in vain. Christ would remain outside of us if he did not also destroy the works of the devil within us—

<meta>catchword: destroy/</meta>

<summary>This page outlines the theological perspective of the 'Listener,' focusing on the necessity of a mystical, internal union with God and the 'death' of the old self through Christ's power. It emphasizes that true religious knowledge must come from internal spiritual enlightenment rather than external sources alone.</summary>

<keywords>Mystical Union, Holy Spirit, New Creature, Indwelling of Christ, Mortification of the Flesh, Inner Enlightenment, Repentance, Baptism, Holy Supper</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Listener (Auditor) and the Preacher (Concionator). It delves deeper into the theological conflict between "essential union" with Christ (the Listener's view) and "justification by imputation" (the Preacher's orthodox view). The Listener emphasizes the necessity of sharing in Christ's life, death, and resurrection experimentally.</meta>

<page-num>7</page-num>

<header>Preface.</header>

[...the Listener] heard that, in short, everything must be held in <term>essential union <note>original: "unione essentiali"; the mystical concept that the believer and Christ are substantially united rather than just legally connected.</note></term>. Namely, being <term>born together <note>original: "connasci"</note></term>, <term>baptized together <note>original: "conbaptizari"</note></term>, <term>dying together <note>original: "commori"</note></term>, <term>crucified together <note>original: "concrucifigi"</note></term>, <term>buried together <note>original: "consepeliri"</note></term>, and <term>rising together <note>original: "conresurgere"</note></term> [with Christ], according to the contents of Apostolic and Prophetic Scripture. Otherwise, Christ’s suffering and death would be in vain and entirely lost upon us. The Head [Christ] and the members [the believers] must by no means be separated from one another. This is what the **Listener**—through the learning of the <term>Anointing <note>A reference to the "unction" or inner teaching of the Holy Spirit mentioned in 1 John 2:27.</note></term>—calls the pure, clean, and clear Gospel, naming it the living knowledge of **JESUS Christ** and the true faith upon which he intends to die and find salvation.

On the other hand, the **Preacher** <note>original: "Concionator"</note> is amazed that his listener uses such unusual speech and puts forward such strange things, since in all his life he has never heard such things from him from the pulpit, nor read them in his writings. Namely, that one wishes to be saved and justified through the <term>indwelling <note>original: "Einwohnung"</note></term> of **JESUS Christ**, and not rather through faith <term>by imputation <note>original: "per imputationem"; the standard Reformation doctrine that Christ's righteousness is legally credited to the believer's account.</note></term>. He claims this is <term>Osiandrian <note>original: "Hosiandrisch"; referring to the teachings of Andreas Osiander (1498–1552), who controversially argued that justification comes from Christ's divine nature dwelling within the believer, rather than a mere legal decree.</note></term> and that it obscures and diminishes the death and merit of Christ. "If we were to be saved by being united with **GOD** through Christ," (says the **Preacher**) "and not <term>by faith through imputation <note>original: "fide per imputationem"</note></term>, then we humans would have to live and walk just as Christ walked, and we would have to follow Him—just as certain <term>Anabaptists <note>original: "Widerteuffer"; a radical Reformation group often criticized by mainstream Lutherans for emphasizing the "imitation of Christ" and outward works of obedience over the internal promise of grace.</note></term> have also taught, and still"

<meta>catchword: teach</meta>

<summary>This page contrasts the 'Listener's' mystical view of a shared life and death with Christ against the 'Preacher's' traditional view of justification through legal imputation. The Preacher warns that the Listener's ideas sound like the controversial Osiandrian or Anabaptist heresies.</summary>

<keywords>essential union, imputation, Andreas Osiander, Anabaptists, justification, indwelling of Christ, imitation of Christ, mystical union</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the debate between the Preacher (representing Lutheran orthodoxy) and the Listener (representing a more mystical, "spiritualist" view). The previous page established the Listener’s belief in an "essential union" with Christ’s life and suffering. Here, the Preacher counters with the doctrine of justification by faith alone and warns against the "inner word" associated with radical reformers like Müntzer and Schwenckfeld.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>

<header>Preface.</header>

...allowed to teach. We cannot do it; Christ has done it all for us. What use would the death and merit of Christ be if we were expected to live as he lived? That is: to be with him in patience—mocked, despised, scorned, indeed hated, persecuted, and killed? That is to say: we need do nothing more but only believe that he died for us and fulfilled the entire Law on our behalf, thereby stilling his Father’s wrath which hung over us all. In this we should comfort ourselves in all distress, with true faith against death, sin, the devil, and hell. For Christ is and stands for us. **If God is for us, who can be against us?** <note>original Latin: "Si Deus pro nobis, quis contra nos" (Romans 8:31).</note> That is: we drink on his tab <note>An idiomatic expression (German: "wir zechen auff seine Kreide") meaning to live or feast on someone else’s credit; here, it refers to relying entirely on Christ’s merits.</note>; **imputed righteousness** <term>imputativa justitia <note>The theological doctrine that a believer is declared righteous because Christ’s perfection is legally "credited" to them, rather than the believer becoming inherently perfect themselves.</note></term> is enough for us, etc.

Secondly, the **Preacher** <term>Concionatorem <note>Latin for "preacher" or "one who addresses an assembly."</note></term> is also greatly amazed at how his **Listener** <term>Auditor <note>Latin for "listener" or "student."</note></term> comes to speak of the **Anointing** <note>A reference to the "inner unction" of the Holy Spirit (1 John 2:27), often cited by mystics as a source of knowledge superior to external preaching.</note>, the "inner Word," and "inner hearing," since he has never heard such things from the pulpit in his entire life, and they are entirely unusual and have never been taught in any university. He says: it is the teaching of **Thomas Müntzer** and **Caspar Schwenckfeld** <note>Müntzer and Schwenckfeld were radical reformers who emphasized the "Inner Light" or the Holy Spirit's direct work in the soul, often clashing with Martin Luther’s emphasis on the written and preached Word.</note>, who allowed themselves to be taught by the Spirit, but they have been labeled as heretics and condemned by all universities, both orally and in writing, as **Enthusiasts** <term>Enthusiasten <note>From the Greek 'entheos' (God within); a derogatory term for those claiming direct divine inspiration.</note></term> and **Fanatics** <term>Schwermer <note>A German term used by Luther to describe those he believed were "swarming" with wild, subjective spiritual ideas unsupported by Scripture.</note></term>. One should be content with the preached Word from the pulpit and with the commentaries on the Bible; one must not flutter about in heaven, [waiting] for illumination...

<meta>catchword: waiting-</meta>

<summary>The Preacher argues that believers should rely solely on Christ's "imputed righteousness" rather than attempting to imitate His suffering, dismissing the Listener's focus on "inner" revelation as dangerous heresy associated with radical reformers.</summary>
<keywords>imputed righteousness, justification by faith, Thomas Müntzer, Caspar Schwenckfeld, inner word, Enthusiasts, Lutheran orthodoxy, Anointing</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the theological debate from page 8. The Preacher (Concionator) has been arguing for a "faith-alone" perspective, while the Listener (Auditor) has been advocating for a more mystical, internal transformation. This page introduces a third figure: Death (Mors), who intervenes to challenge the Preacher's orthodox views.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>

<header>Preface.</header>

<sig>B</sig>

...wait, as the Heavenly Prophets <note>A group of radical reformers in the 1520s who claimed to receive direct revelations from the Holy Spirit rather than through the written Bible.</note> and some Anabaptists <term>Widertäuffer <note>A radical movement that rejected infant baptism and emphasized an "inner" spiritual life, often persecuted by both Catholics and mainstream Protestants.</note></term> have done. Finally, the **Preacher** also refutes the poor Listener's basis for the crucifixion and the putting to death of the flesh. He says: certain monks brought such teachings into the world; one should not thereby diminish or nullify Christ's suffering, cross, and death, as if Christ's death were not enough for the sins of the whole world, and as if we still had to crucify ourselves and earn salvation, he says. We are able to earn nothing by it, even if we martyred ourselves to death. It is an Anabaptist and monkish teaching called **mortification of the flesh** <note>original Latin: "Mortificatio carnis"; the practice of self-denial to subdue physical desires.</note>. Instead, one should be of good cheer in banquets, drinking-bouts, social gatherings, and feasts of joy—**rejoicing in the Lord** <note>original Latin: "gaudete in Domino," a reference to Philippians 4:4.</note>, etc. And one should not make oneself weary and sorrowful through the crucifixion of the body. It is enough that we have Christ, who was crucified and killed for us; He alone did it and had to do it; we need do nothing nor earn anything. 

At this point, a third person enters—namely, **Death** <note>original Latin: "Mors." In this allegorical dialogue, Death acts as a source of ultimate truth that strips away worldly pretension.</note>, the person sent by Christ to all social ranks and over all people under the sun. Death proves that the **Preacher** is blind and does not know what Christ, faith, baptism, the Lord's Supper <term>Nachtmal <note>The German term for the Eucharist or Holy Communion.</note></term>, etc., truly are. He has preached Christ only by half, and in his whole life has never recognized what the crucified and risen Christ truly is...

<meta>catchword: possesses (besidt)</meta>

<summary>The Preacher rejects the need for personal suffering or "mortification," labeling it a legalistic monkish error, while the character of Death enters to accuse the Preacher of having a shallow, incomplete understanding of Christ.</summary>

<page-num>10</page-num>

<header># Preface.</header>

[Death] thus confirms the opinion of the **Listener** <note>original Latin: *Auditoris*</note>, that the **indwelling** <term>Einwohnung <note>The mystical belief that Christ literally lives within the soul of the believer, a core tenet of radical Pietism and mysticism.</note></term> is necessary, and that the believer must die with Christ, according to the meaning of Baptism and of the Lord’s Supper; so that also no one, without hearing the **inner Word** <note>A spiritualist concept where God speaks directly to the heart, often contrasted with the "dead letter" of the written Bible.</note> and the **Anointing** <note>original: *Salbung*; a reference to the spiritual "unction" or insight described in 1 John 2:27.</note>, may know or retain Christ according to His power, effect, and life. Therefore, [Death] has the power and command to send such a **blind leader** <note>A biblical allusion to Matthew 15:14, here directed at the "Preacher" who lacks internal spiritual experience.</note> into eternal darkness, together with all his followers. Therefore take heed, all you people, among all languages, nations, and heathens: whoever accepts this book and lives according to it, God accepts them. Whoever rejects and damns this book, God will damn them. If you promote it to be printed, then God will promote you to life; if you hinder and suppress it, then God will suppress you, whether you be
->equally high or low in the<-
->world.<-

The author ends with a prophetic warning, claiming divine consequences for those who either support or suppress the publication of this book.</summary>

### The First Chapter.

### The Listener rejoices to be essentially united with GOD through Christ, which the Preacher recognizes and judges to be an error.

AUDITOR. Oh, what a love God the Father has shown us, that we human beings should be <term>bodily <note>original Greek: *sōmatikōs*</note></term>—that is, physically—united with Him through His Son Jesus Christ; He in us and we in Him for all eternity. 

CONC. <note>abbreviation for *Concionator*, Latin for "Preacher."</note> Yes, truly it is a great love that the eternal God loves us human beings in the Beloved <note>Referring to Jesus Christ.</note>, which happens to us all by grace through faith, as the Apostle says: By grace you are saved, and that not of yourselves; it is the gift of God, so that no one should boast. 

AUDITOR. The more I reflect on this wonderful union, the more I rejoice in it from the bottom of my heart with great amazement. 

CONC. Yes, it is well worth rejoicing and wondering that God's Son has taken on our flesh and become a child of humanity for our sake, so that through Him we might become children of God. 

AUDITOR. It does not seem to me <margin>Luke 1. John 1.</margin> that the eternal GOD took on Adam's flesh <note>The "flesh of Adam" refers to the fallen, sinful nature of humanity inherited from the first man.</note>, He who was conceived through the Holy Spirit in the pure, chaste Virgin Mary, as we then sing: Not of man's blood nor of flesh, but alone of the Holy Spirit, has God's Word become a human being. 

CONC. Christ is from the seed of David, from <margin>Romans 2. <note>The text cites Romans 2, but the Preacher is likely referencing Romans 1:3–4, which discusses Christ's descent from David according to the flesh.</note></margin> our flesh and blood, as the Apostle says: a Son of God according to the Spirit, and from the seed of David according to the flesh. Therefore He is true God according to the Spirit and true man according to the flesh from Adam. 

AUDITOR. May your Reverence <note>original: *Würde*; a formal title of respect for a member of the clergy.</note> bear with me,

<meta>catchword: keep/</meta>

<summary>The dialogue begins with the Listener arguing for a mystical, bodily union with God, while the Preacher attempts to ground the conversation in orthodox theology regarding grace and the humanity of Christ. The Listener introduces a controversial idea that Christ's flesh did not originate from Adam's fallen nature but from the Holy Spirit.</summary>

<keywords>mystical union, Incarnation, grace vs. works, celestial flesh, Auditor, Preacher, Christology, Davidic descent</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Auditor and the Preacher. The Auditor begins by asserting that the spiritual rebirth of a Christian must mirror the unique, non-Adamic birth of Christ. This leads into a debate over the "Celestial Flesh" doctrine—the idea that Christ's physical nature was not inherited from Adam’s fallen lineage but was a new creation of the Holy Spirit.</meta>

->Christian Dialogue<-

...hold; I speak as if to my <term>pastor <note>original: *Seelsorger*, literally "soul-carer"</note></term>. Everything depends on correctly understanding the birth of Jesus Christ from the Virgin. ## Christian Dialogue

...if I laid this down and brought such strange speeches to the pulpit as I have just heard from you, would they not denounce me as a **fanatic** <term>Schwermer: a derogatory term used by mainstream reformers to describe radical groups who claimed direct revelation from the Holy Spirit</term> and likely even drive me out of the country?

And that is faith: namely, Christ’s life ruling in us, His Spirit in us, His flesh and blood in us. ### Christian Dialogue

...dwell in us, and we in Him. We believe and know that Adam is bodily within us—which we cannot deny when we look at the fruits [of sin]—and he must perish and die within us. <note>See Romans 6, regarding the death of the "old man."</note> Why then should Christ not also be in us, through whom the old man must be and is being killed? This means: without my bodily indwelling; if you do not remain in me and I in you, you are neither righteous nor blessed. **"Apart from me"** <note>original Greek: χωρὶς ἐμῦ, Choris emu</note> stands in the text; that is, **separated from me, apart from me** <note>original Latin: separatè à me, seorsim à me</note>. If I might speak thus: if you were separated from me and remained outside of me, like a cut-off branch, you could bring forth no fruit. This fruit is life, light, righteousness, and salvation; you could not be saved. Since the Heavenly Father is the vinedresser, and Christ the vine, and we Christians the branches, I cannot believe otherwise than what the Scripture testifies: that we must dwell in God and God in us. This is the eternal honor, the heavenly marriage—that we remain bound and linked with God. Since we are born and drawn from God himself, from his side <note>a traditional reference to the Church being born from the wound in Christ's side, as Eve was from Adam's</note>, we also remain in him <margin>Colossians 1</margin> who gave us birth. And this is called faith—namely, Christ dwelling in us—and this is love—namely, God and his Holy Spirit in us. God is our heaven, and we are his heaven. No one goes to heaven except he who has come from heaven and is in heaven—namely, Christ and all Christians.

--- Page 21 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preacher's explanation of the different modes of God's presence, countering the Auditor's belief in an "essential" indwelling within the believer. The Preacher is categorizing presence into four distinct degrees to safeguard the unique nature of Christ's incarnation.</meta>

->### Christian Dialogue<-

...is God present generally among all creatures, so that he may sustain them as long as he wills; this is the first degree of the Divine indwelling, as it is also written in Jeremiah 23: "Am I not God, who fills heaven and earth?" and so on. 

The second degree of Divine indwelling and presence is when God dwells with the angels and humanity and fills them with light and life; and although he does not depart from them, he is nonetheless only with them by way of companionship, just as one neighbor dwells with another, or one good friend beside another. 

The third degree of Divine presence or indwelling is when God dwells with the **newborn** <note>referring to the "born again" believer</note> people and sustains not only their life, but kindles and stirs within them **new movements** <note>original Latin: *novos motus*</note>, which are good impulses through his inspiration. This does not mean that he dwells *inside* David, Jeremiah, Isaiah, or Daniel, but rather he moves their hearts from the outside; **he is present with them separately** <note>original Latin: *adest ipsis separabiliter*; a theological distinction meaning God’s essence remains distinct from the human person’s essence</note>. 

The fourth degree of Divine indwelling is where God dwells bodily in Christ; and this is the only example of **essential indwelling** <note>original Latin: *essentialis inhabitationis*</note>, which is not to be understood as applying to any other creature. Therefore, it is clear and obvious that all the passages of Scripture, which you recount at such length, are not to be understood as the essential indwelling of God in man, but rather as the Divine indwelling or presence.

**AUDITOR.** Such things I was forced to learn in my youth, and had God not let his light shine for me, my soul would have been poorly tended to. May God have mercy on us and open our eyes, so that we may see what is true or a lie.

**CONCIONATOR.** You should surely recognize such wholesome, healthy, and pure teaching as false? Yet our Christian faith stands upon it, as many other writings testify alongside the **Augsburg Confession** <note>The primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church</note>.

**AUDITOR.** I am not a public teacher, as your Worthiness is; therefore I will not judge what is false or right. Yet I say that I do not wish to build my confession of the saving faith upon the sand—that is, upon human books or human teaching—but upon the rock Jesus Christ.

**CONCIONATOR.** The **Augsburg Confession** <note>original Latin: *Augustana Confessio*</note> is our faith, and

### On True Christianity

<note>original: *vom wahren Christenthumb*</note>

[C iii]

...is founded on nothing other than the rock Jesus Christ. **AVD.** Where Christ remains the rock, foundation, and cornerstone, there his teaching and life are not rejected in the slightest, because Christ and his life are inseparable. In these times, people would rather act against the Holy Bible and Christ himself than step away from the **Augsburg Confession** <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*; the primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church, which the Auditor suggests has become more important to people than Christ's own life</note>. That is to say, they let go of Jesus Christ sooner than they do the books of men, because so many princes, lords, counts, knights, nobles, commoners, cities, merchants, and so on, depend and rely upon them. 

**CONC.** You may make of it what you will, yet you will find this doctrine of yours—concerning the **indwelling of God in man**—approved nowhere as being correct, and everyone shrinks away from such talk. 

**AVD.** It is no better for that; if our faith were as large as a mustard seed, we would fear God more than men. Were Christ in us, we would not believe merely to please anyone. 

**CONC.** If such **mutual indwelling** <note>original: *mutua in-habitatio*</note> were to exist, then our entire foundation in the schools and at the pulpit would be false, and we would have to walk as Christ did. His suffering and merit, which occurred for our sins, would thus be nullified and abolished. 

**AVD.** Far be it! <note>original: *Das sey ferne*; a strong biblical idiom of rejection</note> To suggest that the believer should not walk as Christ walked, or that through his life and conduct in Christ, he should despise or diminish Christ's death and merit. On the contrary, his death and merit remain in the memory, and there the power of **Holy Baptism** is exercised. Precisely for this reason he has taken sin away from us: so that we, who have died to sin, should no longer live in it. For if we were to say: "Christ suffered for us so that we might live in sin and walk according to the **old man** <note>referring to the unregenerate, sinful nature of humanity</note>," then the death of Christ would be denied, along with Baptism and the **Lord's Supper** <note>original: *Nachtmal*</note>. 

**CONC.** Christ has come to destroy the works of the devil. He, he, he has done it for us; we can do nothing. We should only believe that he has done it, and through faith comfort ourselves with his merit, and receive the Sacraments for the

### Christian Conversation
<note>original: *Christlich Gespräch*</note>

...God is present generally with all creatures, so that he may preserve them for as long as he wills; that is the first degree of God’s dwelling-with, as it also stands in Jeremiah 23: "Am I not God, I who fill heaven and earth?" etc. The second degree of divine dwelling-with and presence is when God dwells with the angels and men, and fills them with light and life; and although he does not depart from them, he is nevertheless only with them according to fellowship, just as one neighbor dwells by another, or one good friend alongside another. 

The third degree of divine presence or dwelling-with is when God dwells with the newborn <note>referring to those "born again" through spiritual renewal</note> humans; he does not merely sustain their life, but ignites and stirs in them **new movements** <note>original: *novos motus*; good impulses or spiritual stirrings</note>, good inclinations through inspiration. This is not to say that he dwells *within* David, Jeremiah, Isaiah, or Daniel, but rather that from the outside he moves their hearts; **he is present to them separately** <note>original: *adest ipsis separabiliter*; a theological term meaning God remains a separate entity from the person he is influencing</note>. 

The fourth degree of divine dwelling-with is when God dwells bodily in Christ, and this is now the only example of **essential indwelling** <note>original: *essentialis inhabitationis*; the idea that God's very essence is one with the person</note>, which should not be understood as applying to any other creature. Therefore, it is clear and obvious that all the passages of Scripture which you have recounted at length are not to be understood as the essential indwelling of God in man, but rather as the divine "dwelling-with" or presence. 

**AVD.** Such things I was forced to learn in my youth; and had God not let his light shine for me, my soul would have been poorly cared for. May God have mercy on us and open our eyes so that we may see what is true or what is a lie. 

**CONC.** Would you truly recognize such wholesome, healthy, and pure doctrine as false? Yet our Christian faith stands upon it, as do other writings alongside the **Augsburg Confession** <note>the primary confession of faith for the Lutheran Church</note>. 

**AVD.** I am not a public teacher, as your Dignity is; therefore I will not judge what is false or right. Yet I say that I do not wish to build my confession of the saving faith upon sand—that is, upon the books of men or the teachings of men—but rather upon the rock, Jesus Christ. 

**CONC.** The **Augsburg Confession** is our faith, and...

<meta>catchword: stands</meta>

The Auditor rejects this academic categorization, arguing that faith should be based on Christ himself rather than human-made religious confessions.</summary>

...sees nothing else founded upon, than upon the rock Jesus Christ. 

**AUDITOR.** Where Christ remains the rock, foundation, and cornerstone, there his teaching and life will not be rejected in the slightest, because Christ and his life are inseparable. In these times, people would rather act against the Holy Bible and Christ himself than step away from the **Augsburg Confession** <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*; the primary confession of faith for the Lutheran Church, established in 1530</note>. That is to say, they would sooner let go of Christ Jesus than human books, because so many princes, lords, counts, knights, nobles and commoners, cities, merchants, etc., depend and rely upon them. 

**PREACHER.** You may make of it what you will, yet you will find this teaching of yours—regarding the indwelling of God in man—approved nowhere as being correct, and everyone shrinks away from such talk. 

**AUDITOR.** It is no better for that; if faith in us were as large as a mustard seed, we would fear God more than men. If Christ were in us, we would not believe merely to please anyone. 

**PREACHER.** If such **mutual indwelling** <note>original: *mutua inhabitatio*; the theological concept of the believer living in God and God simultaneously living within the believer</note> were to be, then our entire foundation in the schools and in the pulpit would be false, and we would have to walk as Christ did. His suffering and **merit** <note>original: *Verdienst*; the spiritual credit Christ earned through his sacrifice</note>, which happened for our sins, would thus be nullified and abolished. 

**AUDITOR.** Far be it from the believer not to walk as Christ walked, or that through his life and conduct in Christ, he should despise or diminish Christ’s death and merit. Rather, his death and merit remain in memory, where the power of **Holy Baptism** is exercised. And for this very reason he has taken sin away from us: so that we, who have died to sin, should no longer live in it. For if we were to say that Christ suffered for us so that we might live in sin and walk according to the **old man** <note>a biblical term for the sinful, unregenerate nature of a human before spiritual rebirth</note>, then the death of Christ would be denied, along with Baptism and the **Lord's Supper** <note>original: *Nachtmal*; the sacrament of Communion</note>. 

**PREACHER.** Christ came to destroy the works of the devil. He, he, he has done it for us; we can do nothing. We should only believe that he has done it, and comfort ourselves in his merit through faith, and receive the sacraments for salvation...

salvation. I have received such [teachings] from my dear **instructors** <note>original: *Præceptoribus*</note> in the **universities** <note>original: *Academiis*</note>, those places from which one must obtain learned people. You are a **layman** <note>original: *Idiota*; here meaning someone without formal academic or theological training</note> and have not studied as we have, yet you presume to read the Holy Scripture. The authority to interpret Scripture rests with us; it is not commanded to you. You should listen to us, look to us, learn from us, and bring forth no new teachings.

**AUDITOR.** Your Honor would surely grant me that the righteous shall live by his own faith, and that every person must give an account for his own soul, and no stranger can do it for him.

**PREACHER.** I also preach nothing other than faith, and that every person is saved through faith, through **free imputation** <note>original: *per imputationem gratuitam*; the Protestant theological doctrine that God "credits" Christ’s righteousness to a sinner's account, rather than the sinner actually becoming inherently righteous</note>.

**AUDITOR.** Whoever preaches faith preaches the Gospel, or Christ the crucified. Whoever preaches Christ preaches the crucifixion and the **killing of the flesh** <note>original: *Tödtung deß Fleisches*; the mortification of worldly desires</note> and the **making alive of the spirit** <note>original: *Lebendigmachung deß Geistes*</note>, so that we in Christ must die to the "old man" and live to the "new." We must put off the old man and put on the new—that is, the life of Christ—and it must be within the believing person. Thus, faith, Christ, and his life are essentially one and not at all divided, just as God and the Word, or Light and Life, are one thing in a person. The Word is Christ, and it is the Spirit and the Life; and the Life is the Light of men, which shines in the darkness. Just as one cannot separate the Word, Spirit, Light, and Life from one another when they are within a person, one can likewise not divide faith, Christ, and his life. These must be within the believing person and not remain outside him, nor be merely imputed **from the outside** <note>original: *ab extra*</note>.

**PREACHER.** You still insist on remaining in your **fanaticism** <note>original: *Schwermerey*; a derogatory term used by established reformers for those they considered radical or mystical extremists</note>, seeking to become righteous and saved through the **essential indwelling of God** <note>original: *wesentliche Einwohnung Gottes*</note>, which you have neither heard nor learned from me.

**AUDITOR.** Tell me, dear Sir, is it not true that the works of Satan—such as adultery, fornication, hate, envy, wrath, pride, disobedience, and unrighteousness—are within a person and not outside a person? (For what goes in from the outside [only] defiles the man...

# Of True Christianity

...not men. If such works of the Devil are found within us, then Christ's death must also be within us, so that He may destroy those works in our hearts. We must be baptized with Him into a death like His; were He not in us, He could be of no use to us. Christ saves no one from the outside, but through faith from within <note>original: *Christus ab extra nullum salvat, sed per fidem ab intra*</note>; hell damns no one from the outside—even if an angel were among the devils in hell—and heaven saves no one from the outside, but from within <note>original: *Cœlum ab extra nullum salvat, sed ab intra*</note>. Even if a devil came into heaven among the angels, so to speak, it would not save him. Christ the Rock must be in us, and we in Him; for faith is Christ in us. Christ’s kingdom is in us when we walk in Him, and we are in His kingdom. 

Were Christ’s kingdom not in us, we could never pray "Thy kingdom come." Were heaven not in us, we could never enter into heaven. God is our heaven, as mentioned before, and we are His heaven. Christ is made for us by God for righteousness, and for holiness, and for redemption <note>A reference to 1 Corinthians 1:30</note>, so that as it is written: "He who boasts, let him boast in the Lord." In this I rejoice: that I am sanctified, redeemed, and justified in, with, and through Christ, and not outside of Him. In this I rejoice; in this I live; upon this I die. 

**PREACHER.** You are no listener of mine! You do not learn such things from me. You will surely bring me into danger—risking my office and my very life. Away with such talk, with your "Osiandrian" nonsense <note>original: *Hosian-drilmo*; a derogatory pun on the name of Andreas Osiander (1498–1552), a theologian who controversially taught that Christ's divine nature dwells within the believer</note> and this damned **fanaticism** <note>original: *Schwermerey*; a term used by the established church to dismiss mystical or radical spiritual movements as "dreamy" or "deluded"</note>. I shall stick to the sound, pure, and wholesome doctrine of our **instructors** <note>original: *Præceptoren*</note> and to their writings.

## Christian Dialogue

**AUDITOR.** Dear Sir, even if you reject me as an unworthy member who is not fit to read or hear your teachings further, this is still my comfort: that I am not rejected by God. I remain in Him, and I learn more from His Spirit within myself than all the books and sermons in the world might teach me until the Last Day.

**PREACHER.** Why should I not be justly angry with you? You deny <term>imputed righteousness [iustitiam imputativam]</term> <note>The central Protestant doctrine that God "credits" Christ's righteousness to a sinner's account, allowing them to be saved despite their personal flaws.</note>, which is meant to be the comfort of all pious people. For if one does away with imputed righteousness, then all our comfort regarding the precious merit of Jesus Christ is gone. We cannot live without sin; we cannot be pure and without blemish and holy, as your "physical indwelling" <note>The Auditor's belief that Christ literally lives within the believer's heart.</note> requires and demands. As Saint John also testifies when he says: "Whoever says he has no sin makes God a liar, and the truth is not in him." <note>1 John 1:8-10</note> Likewise, David says: "Who is there who does not sin?" <note>A paraphrase of Psalm 143:2 or 1 Kings 8:46.</note> Since sin is in us—which no one can deny—how can your "physical indwelling of Christ" take place, which requires a pure and holy person? The <term>Cathars</term> <note>A medieval sect that sought extreme spiritual purity; the Preacher uses the name here as a slur to imply the Auditor is a heretic.</note> taught this in times past, as did certain Anabaptists.

**AUDITOR.** Listen, dear Sir, to my meaning regarding imputed righteousness. I do not say that a person is without sin, for all saints have sin. And whoever says he has no sin is a liar, and Christ did not come for the sake of the pious and righteous, but for the sake of poor sinners.

Now John says, as mentioned: "Whoever says he has no sin is a liar, and the truth is not in him"—that is to say, Jesus Christ does not dwell in him. But it is written on the other page <note>Refers to 1 John 3:8</note> as follows: "Whoever commits sin is of the Devil and is not born of God." That is: whoever commits sin and yet still wants to have the <term>righteousness of Christ [iustitiam Christi]</term> reckoned to him, he is of the Devil, and a child of the Devil, born of Satan. Thus, to no one the—

<meta>catchword: no one the [nem der]</meta>

<summary>The Preacher accuses the Auditor of heresy for prioritizing Christ's internal indwelling over the legal doctrine of imputed righteousness, while the Auditor argues that claiming Christ's righteousness while continuing to sin is a mark of the Devil.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Preacher (CONC.) and the Auditor (AVD.). # On True Christianity
[D]
<meta>catchword: none</meta>

...to none is Death <note>Original: "der Todt" – referring here to the benefits of Christ’s death being applied to the soul.</note> reckoned but to the believer; yet no one believes in Jesus Christ unless he stops letting sin rule over him—that is, if he bathes in vices and sins without any inner resistance. There is thus a great distinction between **having** sin and **committing** sin. All saints **have** sin, and they confess this in their "Our Father" <note>The Lord's Prayer, specifically the plea for forgiveness.</note> daily and willingly; but they do not **commit** sin—that is, they do not persist in the consent to continue in sin, or as Paul says, they do not let sin rule in their mortal body. For if the Spirit of Christ dwells in them, they would otherwise lack the witness of Jesus and have no faith.

**CON.** So you still hold onto your **essential indwelling**? <note>The Auditor’s controversial belief that Christ’s actual substance lives within the believer, rather than Christ merely "counting" them as righteous legally.</note>

**AVD.** How could it be otherwise? If I denied or lost this essential indwelling, I would have no saving faith; I would not be born of God; I would not have the grace of God through Jesus Christ within me. Indeed, I could not be saved, for true salvation lies only in the **essential union** of man with God in Christ Jesus, and in Christ dwelling in our hearts, rooted through love.

**CONC.** How then can the righteous, holy Christ and the sinful man exist together in one person, and how would **imputed righteousness** <term>imputativa justitia</term> <note>The Protestant doctrine that God "credits" Christ's perfection to a sinner's account.</note> be maintained?

**AVD.** Oh, very well indeed! For where a person recognizes and confesses their indwelling sin, there the grace of God—which is Christ—fits perfectly. But where a person considers themselves righteous and without sin, there is neither faith nor Christ. As the Apostle testifies: where sin is powerful, there the grace of God is much more powerful. Whatever good is now in such a person is not their own, but belongs to Jesus Christ, who dwells and rules in them. To such a person, Christ’s death, suffering, merit, and righteousness are reckoned or **imputed** <term>imputirt</term>. But to one who lets sin rule in their body—that is, one who *commits* sin, as John says—Christ’s merit or righteousness is in no way **imputed** <term>imputiert</term>. The reason is that there is no remorse, repentance, or recognition of sin through the grace of God, and because Christ remains on the outside...

<summary>The Auditor distinguishes between "having sin" (human nature) and "committing sin" (willful surrender to vice), arguing that Christ's indwelling is necessary for true faith. He claims that imputed righteousness only applies to those who struggle against sin, rather than those who let it rule them.</summary>
<keywords>essential indwelling, imputed righteousness, iustitia imputativa, original sin, repentance, union with God, Paul, Apostle John</keywords>

# Christian Dialogue
<meta>catchword: the</meta>

...makes no one righteous or saved from the outside, but from within through faith. When the old man, **Adam**, rules in us—that is, when we commit sin and live for sins—then the inner new man, **Christ**, is dead. Conversely, when the inner new man **Christ** rules—that is, when we feel and confess sin, but do not do it—then Adam has died in us, and no **imputation from the outside** <term>imputatio ab extra</term> <note>The theological concept that righteousness is "credited" to a person's account by God from the outside, rather than being an internal change of nature.</note> helps there.

**CON.** If you were a teacher or preacher, you would lead people astray with such unusual talk of the inner and outer man. We are not accustomed to speaking this way; we do not find it in the **Augsburg Confession** <term>Confessio Augustana</term> <note>The primary confession of faith of the Lutheran Church, established in 1530 as a standard of orthodoxy.</note>, by which we must all be guided, nor in the writings of the old and new teachers.

The Apostle says: Though our outer-

# Of True Christianity
D ij
<meta>catchword: simple-hearted</meta>

...everything external is dead and in vain.

**CONCIONATOR.** You say the **Anointing** <note>original: "Salbung." A reference to 1 John 2:27, implying direct spiritual instruction from the Holy Spirit rather than human teachers.</note> has taught you this, and yet no preacher or interpreter of Scripture preaches and teaches in this way; thus you hold the **oral preaching office** <term>Predigampt</term> <note>The formal institution of the clergy preaching the Word, which the established church viewed as the primary means of grace.</note> as nothing, even though faith must come from hearing, as the Apostle says.

**AUDITOR.** The Apostle makes a twofold office: namely, the **Office of the Spirit**, where the servant—taught and sent by God—preaches the Spirit, the power, and the life, and conducts his conduct, teaching, and life like Christ; and the **Office of the Letter**, where the servant learns from and is chosen by men, and preaches only the bare, dead letter without power or vitality <note>original: "Krafft vnnd Safft," literally "power and juice/sap." A favorite phrase of spiritualist writers to describe the lack of spiritual life in formalist preaching.</note>, without Spirit and life. Whoever now hears those taught by God or sent by God with a good heart does not come away empty, so that the saying of Paul is true: **Faith comes from hearing** <note>original: "Fides ex auditu." Romans 10:17.</note>, namely from the inner hearing. But whoever hears the servant of the letter will gain nothing from it, and there faith cannot come from hearing, for the outer testimony will not harmonize with the inner.

**CONC.** Paul is speaking of the Old Testament, where the servants under **Moses** preached the dead letter. But we in the **New Testament** under Christ preach no dead letter, but rather carry out the **Office of the Spirit**, which is the New Testament.

**AUD.** I wish from my heart that I could find one among so many thousands who, taught and sent by God, would carry out the **Office of the Spirit** and preach **Christ crucified** from the pulpit.

**CONC.** Do not speak so boldly <note>original: "milte." Likely used here to mean "freely" or "improperly."</note>; we all indeed preach the **Holy Gospel**; therefore you should hold to the oral word, for there you would be rightly instructed and would not need to wait for the Spirit.

**AUD.** All pious people hear sermons out of obedience, some out of habit, some out of fear of worldly punishment and so that they are not looked upon as if they wanted to tear down good order; but therein they truly find neither power nor vitality. The bare **history of Christ** <note>original: "bloſſe Hiſtoria." This refers to an intellectual or historical knowledge of Jesus's life that lacks personal, spiritual transformation.</note> is preached without any vitality or power; and where the Holy Spirit the simple-hearted...

The Preacher defends the established oral ministry as the proper channel for the New Testament Gospel.</summary>

# Christian Dialogue
<meta>catchword: it is:</meta>

...did not teach simple, good hearts, then no human would be saved through your teaching and preaching.

**CONCIONATOR.** The Holy Spirit will not come from heaven and teach you as happened before Christ’s coming, when He taught the Prophets. Instead, God has ordained that one human shall teach another; through humans He wills His Word to be preached, and not through the Spirit. Whoever now despises the **preaching office** <term>Predigampt</term> <note>The institutional role of the clergy in delivering sermons and sacraments.</note> and waits for the Spirit is an **enthusiast** <term>Enthusiasta</term>, a **fanatic** <term>Schwermer</term> <note>original: "Schwermer." A derogatory term used by mainstream reformers like Luther to describe those who claimed direct revelation from God outside of the Bible.</note>, and is deceived by the evil spirit. We have nothing else but that which is contained in the writings of the Prophets and Apostles. **To my law** <note>original: "Ad legem meam." Likely a reference to Isaiah 8:20, "To the law and to the testimony!"</note> it is said; one must hold to the written Word and expect nothing from the spirit; the spirit gives rise to fanaticism, as we have always experienced.

**AUDITOR.** I do not despise the oral preaching office at all, and the written Word is very dear to me, yet not without the Spirit. The external testimony comes into agreement with my heart through the Holy Spirit; it is the **Law of God**, which is my light and life and my salvation. It is the Word of the Father and is in our hearts and the mouth of the inner man. Nor does the Holy Spirit need to come from heaven; He has long been in heaven, which is in us, for **the love of God is poured out in our hearts through the Holy Spirit** <note>original: "die Liebe Gottes ist ausgegossen in vnsern Hertzen durch den H. Geist." A citation of Romans 5:5.</note>, whom He has given us. He is the Word and Law of God, from which all Prophetic and Apostolic books were dictated and written.

**CONC.** Have you, in all your life, heard such things from me from the **pulpit** <term>Cantzel</term> or read them in an interpretation of the Bible?

**AVD.** I must confess that I did not learn it from humans, nor from the pulpit, nor in any interpretation of **Scripture** <term>Schrifft</term>. But God does not forget the simple-hearted; He makes them more learned than all their teachers.

**CONC.** All universities reject and condemn, both orally and in writing, the **self-taught** <term>autodidaktous</term> <note>original: "αὐτοδιδάκτους." A Greek term for those who are "taught by themselves" or directly by God, rather than through formal schooling.</note>, that is, those who teach themselves; and they say, not without reason, that these are headstrong people. They also forbid throughout the lands and kingdoms that anyone should mention the **inner Word** from the pulpit,

# Of True Christianity

...whether it be Enthusiastic, **Schwenkfeldian** <note>original: "Schwenckfeldisch." Refers to the followers of Caspar Schwenkfeld, who prioritized the "Inner Word" of the Spirit over external sacraments and literal scripture.</note>, etc. and you come thirsting along with the **inner Word** <term>innern Wort</term>, inner hearing, and the teaching of the Spirit.

**AUDITOR.** I am no **self-taught person** <note>original: "αὐτοδίδακτος" (autodidaktos)</note>; I do not learn these things from myself, but from Him who is within me. Namely, from God I hear and learn these Divine things. Whoever rejects the inner Word damns and rejects the **third Article of Faith** <note>Refers to the section of the Apostles' Creed concerning the Holy Spirit, the church, and sanctification.</note> regarding Sanctification or **Enlightenment** <term>Erleuchtung</term>, and Jesus Christ Himself, who is the Word, Light, and Life in all people. Is not the Scripture written through the inspiration of the Holy Spirit? Is not the **Letter** <note>The literal, written text of the Bible as opposed to its spiritual meaning.</note> a veil or curtain of the Word? I say, if the inner Word were not, there would be no Scripture, no Christ—indeed, no creature could exist or live.

**CONCIONATOR.** You hear well that one must abide by the decrees and writings of the **Universities** <term>Hohen Schulen</term>, which, with the help of the **Secular Authority** <term>Weltlichē Oberkeit</term> as their organs or tools, condemn and forbid such teaching.

**AUDITOR.** God has struck them with blindness because they quench the Spirit; and because they deny the Light within them, He has let darkness befall them in return, so that they neither see nor know what they condemn. Yet they themselves write in their books thus: *In vain does the tongue of the teacher labor from without, unless there is one within who teaches.* <note>original: "frustrà lingua docentis laborat ab extra, nisi sit intus qui doceat." A famous sentiment often attributed to St. Augustine, emphasizing that human preaching is useless without the Holy Spirit's internal work.</note> That is, he teaches or preaches in vain from the pulpit, from the outside, where the Holy Spirit does not teach in the heart. A man might hear a sermon until the Last Day, yet he will not come to Christ unless he be **drawn by the Spirit of the Father** <note>A reference to John 6:44.</note>—that is, unless the Holy Spirit enlightens him from within.

**CONCIONATOR.** I might almost be moved by you to allow for the inner Word alongside the outer.

**AUDITOR.** Ah, would to GOD that you and all others together would submit yourselves to the **Anointing** <term>Salbung</term> <note>referring to 1 John 2:27, the "anointing" that teaches believers all things.</note>, the inner Word, and read the Holy Bible only as a testimony; you would come to the living knowledge of Jesus Christ, where no error or darkness can be.

**CONCIONATOR.** Indeed, with the help of God, we also have the knowledge of Christ and the pure Truth.

**AUDITOR.** Without the drawing of the Father,

# Christian Dialogue

...learns;
that is / without the inner Word it is impossible for one to rightly recognize Christ / to preach the Truth / etc. For whoever does not hear or learn it from the Father / does not come to the Son. 

**CONCIONATOR.** When I have the Bible / I surely have God's Word and know no other Word / than the writings of the Prophets and Apostles / which are finely, clearly, and briefly interpreted and explained by our teachers. 

**AUDITOR.** Yes / according to the letter and external testimony one has God’s Word / but not everyone has the understanding / unless we are then taught by God through the **Anointing** <term>Salbung</term> <note>A reference to 1 John 2:27, implying an internal spiritual guidance that teaches the believer.</note>, which is within us. The Word lies 

<margin>1.</margin> hidden in three places. 1. In the letter of the Scripture / but no one understands the Scripture / unless he is taught by God. 

<margin>2.</margin> 2. It lies hidden in the flesh <note>Refers to the Incarnation, the Word made flesh in Jesus Christ.</note> / no one recognizes it unless he is 

<margin>3.</margin> drawn by the Father. 3. It lies in the heart / no one finds it / except the believer enlightened by the Holy Spirit.

**AUDITOR.** What they or others do / I do not know / for they do not allow us to have their books / so that we might see from them / how it stands with them. I believe they reject the oral office of preaching as little as I do. For the inner Word makes the external testimony of the **Ministry of the Spirit** <note>original: "Ministerii Spiritus"</note> more certain and more recognizable / and they come together in the **hearts of the believers** <note>original: "cordibus Credentium"</note>. How then could one reject the spoken Word? 

**CONCIONATOR.** I have explicitly read in their books / that they hold nothing of the preached word / but rather they create an internal Word and hearing / which we, along with all the **Universities** <term>hohen Schulen</term>, do not permit. 

**AUDITOR.** They create no inner Word nor hearing / rather it is already there without their making or setting it / and it remains true into eternity / whoever does not hear and learn it from the Father...

# On True Christianity

...which have been accepted by many princes and lands, counts, cities, etc. I stick to the multitude; that which is defended and protected everywhere. For whenever one is heard to notice anything of the inner Word, or Hearing, of the inspiration of the Spirit, of the invisible Church and all peoples, then one immediately cries out against him as a fanatic <term>Schwermer</term> <note>Literally "one who swarms"; a derogatory term used by mainstream reformers for radical groups who claimed direct inspiration from the Holy Spirit.</note>, a heretic, a deceiver, a **Schwenkfelder** <note>A follower of Caspar Schwenkfeld, who emphasized the "inner Word" and the spiritual nature of the sacraments.</note>, an **Anabaptist** <term>Widerteuffer</term>, a **Heavenly Prophet** <note>A term often applied to radical reformers like Andreas Karlstadt who claimed prophetic visions.</note>, and he is hardly safe in his life, etc.

### The Third Chapter.

### That the mortification of the flesh is necessary through
### the death of Christ in us, which death is our life; the
### Preacher, however, comforts himself with the death of Christ from the outside, through
### imputed righteousness <term>zugerechnete Gerechtigkeit</term> <note>The Lutheran doctrine that a believer is declared righteous by God because of Christ's merit, rather than becoming inherently sinless.</note>.

**AUDITOR.** Every day I rejoice with wonder that we believers or baptized ones are purchased from the earth through the death of Christ, and can have our walk above in heaven, while we mortify the members that are on earth. **CONC.** Yes, if the death of Christ were in us—that is, if Christ were in us—then we would live entirely without sin, just as he himself; thus we would also be in heaven, as he was when he walked among us on earth and said: No one goes to heaven but he who has come from heaven, namely the Son of Man who is in heaven <note>original: "nemo ascendit in coelum, nisi qui descendit de coelo, filius hominis qui est in coelo." Refers to John 3:13.</note>. But we are full of sins and are not able to live in Christ until after our death. **AUD.** That we have sin, no one denies; but that we would want to commit sin—that is, to let sin rule in us—that is not fitting for any believer or baptized person. For the reborn cannot sin; that is, they cannot commit sins by persevering in them against their conscience <note>original Latin: "Nam renati peccare non possunt, id est, non possunt facere peccata perseverando in iis contra conscientiam."</note>. Otherwise, there would be no faith, and baptism and the death of Christ would be entirely denied. **CONC.** I notice well what you want; you mix one thing into another; you maintain that the indwelling of Christ...

...or His death, and faith, and baptism are one thing; which is a strange teaching and a delusion to me and all the universities <term>hohen Schulen</term> <note>Literally "high schools," referring to the established academic centers of theology.</note>. **AUDITOR.** **Where faith is, there also is the baptism into the death of Christ, and the death of Christ is Christ and His life. For His death is the life of all who believe and are baptized.** Therefore our old body must go to the cross with Christ—that is, our members on earth must be put to death in us, through the death of Christ in us; thus we walk in heaven, in GOD, in love, and in the Spirit, which God has given us. **PREACHER.** You understand this discourse to refer to the earthly old man, to the old creature who walked according to the lusts of the flesh; and heaven to refer to the newborn man who dwells in God through Christ Jesus. **AUDITOR.** The believing man has two earths within him: the deadly, dark, damnable earth from the first birth through Adam; the other earth is the new earth from the second birth through Christ. If Christ's flesh and blood are in us—which we also receive in the Supper <term>Nachtmahl</term> in memory of the death of Christ—then the body must become new and different. The soul does not need to become something else; it will certainly be renewed and enlightened. **We must be reborn in the body through the death of Christ. Out of death comes renewal, and not without death.** Thus it is fulfilled: "Behold, I create new heavens and earths." **PREACHER.** You will not find these things in the books of Master Philip <note>Referring to Philip Melanchthon (1497–1560), the primary systematic theologian of the Lutheran Reformation.</note>, nor in the writings of Luther, much less in the **Augsburg Confession** <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*; the central document of the Lutheran faith presented in 1530.</note> or other writings; nor has any university ever taught this way. It makes me wonder how you bring forth such strange things, seeing as they are not preached from any pulpit in the whole world. **AUDITOR.** Whoever would not accept such things would be rejecting the new birth, the second new creation through the Son, and Christ would not be in him. He would have no faith and would lack heavenly citizenship; he would be no Christian. For our soul or spirit must indeed be fed with Christ’s flesh and blood—that is, it must be clothed...

as He says: whoever does not eat my flesh and does not drink my blood cannot be saved <note>A reference to John 6:53.</note>. And we must become embodied with Christ’s crucified body in Baptism <term>Tauffe</term> and the Lord’s Supper <term>Nachtmal</term>. We put on the Lord Jesus Christ; through such a body of Christ, our old body from the earth is killed and crucified, and we are no longer <margin>Rom. 6.</margin> carnally minded, but spiritually. Because God's Spirit dwells in us. For whoever does not have the Spirit of Christ does not belong to Him, and would not be His "purchased one" from the earth into the heavenly being. Such nourishment through Christ’s flesh and blood, both inward and outward, is no shadow, work, nor ghost, but a lovely essence—that is, new heavens and earth within the human being. For the external new heaven and earth would be of no use to us if it were not all also within us. Thus our body is dead for the sake of Christ’s indwelling, and the spirit lives for the sake of righteousness; indeed, Christ’s death in us is our life. Therefore we abide in God and God in us; we abide in the Spirit and the Spirit in us; we abide in Christ and Christ in us; we abide in love and love in us. But God Himself is love, poured into our hearts through the Holy Spirit which He has given us; this is the heavenly citizenship—that we dwell in heaven. There, everything is together and must not be divided from one another. 

**PREACHER.** <note>The abbreviation "Conc." stands for "Concionator," the Latin term for Preacher.</note> Such a killing of the members on earth through the indwelling of Christ is "Repentance" or "Justification" <term>Rechtfertigung</term> according to you. But for me and the orthodox <note>original: "Orthodoxis"</note> in all the universities <term>hohen Schulen</term> <note>Literally "high schools," referring to the established academic centers of theology.</note>, no such patchwork or confusion is made. We orderly set down three parts in the conversion of a person or for justification. The first is the recognition of sin <note>original: "agnitio peccati"</note> and contrition <note>original: "Contritio"</note>, where a person bears remorse and sorrow for their sins, or where they confess all their sins before their pastor <term>Seelsorger</term> <note>Literally "soul-carer."</note>. The second part of repentance is faith <note>original: "Fides"</note>, a trust and confidence in the death of Christ that occurred on the Cross for all sins, where a person, by their own strength, grasps a firm belief from the Scriptures. The third part is new obedience <note>original: "nova obedientia"</note> or satisfaction <note>original: "satisfactio"</note>...

# Christian Conversation

Such pure, healthy, and wholesome teaching on repentance, etc., is elegantly, roundly, and briefly grasped and drawn together by our teachers <note>original: "præceptoribus"</note> in the <term>Augsburg Confession</term> <note>original: "Augustanam Confessionem," the primary statement of faith for the Lutheran reformation.</note>, in the examination of those to be ordained <note>original: "examen ordinandorum"</note>, in the body of doctrine <note>original: "corpus Doctrinæ"</note>, and in many other writings which I cannot all recount, which God has brought forth through enlightened, learned men, and will yet bring forth.

Then the effective power and life of Christ are denied. Faith, love, and the Spirit are missed, and no renewal or improvement of life can follow in the whole world, as is evident before our eyes. **For Christ and His saving indwelling are unknown** <note>original: "Nam ignoratur Christus & ejus salvifica inhabitatio"</note>; that is, people know nothing of Christ, nor of His essential indwelling—which is the Kingdom of God within us—and

<meta>This page continues a dialogue between a Preacher (CON.) and an Auditor (AVC./AUD.). The Auditor is critiquing the systematic, academic theology taught in the universities, while the Preacher defends his adherence to the established confessions of the church.</meta>

# On True Christianity
<page-num>39</page-num>

**AUDITOR:** I cannot express what harm and misery, distress and blindness, come over the whole world from such teaching that you carry out in all the <term>universities</term> <note>original: "hohen Schulen," referring to the high schools or universities where clergy were trained.</note>.

**PREACHER:** From this teaching I cannot depart, even unto death. I have taken an oath to remain with these books; I have also signed my name to them. Should I abandon them, I would be branded a heretic and driven out of the land. For consider that the heads of the world—such as the Emperor, King, Princes, Counts, Lords, Knights, the noble and ignoble, rich and poor, servants and free men, lawyers and merchants—all accept and believe exactly what I write and preach.

**AUDITOR:** That does not matter; faith is not everyone's affair. If Christ were known among all these social ranks, He would also have His rule and lordship in you and in them, and you would all hear His voice and be willing to suffer for it. I have indeed also signed my name several times for compelling reasons, but I took no oath upon the books of men by doing so. Rather, I pledged myself with such a signature to remain with the writings of the Prophets and Apostles, and never to depart from them. If I find something alongside them in the writings of teachers that is consistent with the Apostolic teaching, I will gladly accept it. But that I should swear an oath on the books of men—let that be far from me! No teacher shall hold so much weight with me that I would want to die for him—as some hang so tightly onto the Pope, onto **Luther**, onto **Philip** <note>Philipp Melanchthon (1497–1560), a key German Reformer and collaborator with Luther.</note>, onto **Zwingli** <note>Huldrych Zwingli (1484–1531), leader of the Reformation in Switzerland.</note>, onto **Schwenckfeld** <note>Caspar Schwenckfeld (1489–1561), a radical Reformer who emphasized inner spirituality over outward forms.</note>, onto **Osiander** <note>Andreas Osiander (1498–1552), a controversial Lutheran theologian known for his views on "inner" righteousness.</note>, onto **Muhammad**, and other men, that they even allow themselves to be hunted and killed over it. No, not so for me; I cling to **Jesus Christ**, and I remain with the writings of the Prophets and Apostles until death.

**PREACHER:** Do you not cling to the teachers? Why then have you signed their books just as I have?

**AUDITOR:** I did not sign my name to their teaching or the books of men. Rather, because they set their <term>intent</term> <note>original: "Intent," meaning their aim or primary purpose.</note> upon the Apostolic Scripture and [presented] the same to all people...

<summary>The Auditor argues for a faith centered solely on Christ and Scripture, rejecting the rigid oaths taken by clergy to follow specific human teachers or denominational "books of men." He lists various religious leaders—from Luther to Muhammad—as examples of figures people wrongly die for instead of focusing on the Apostles.</summary>

<keywords>Scripture vs. Tradition, religious oaths, Martin Luther, Philipp Melanchthon, Huldrych Zwingli, Caspar Schwenckfeld, Andreas Osiander, authority of the Apostles, academic theology</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Auditor’s explanation for why he signed church documents (the "subscription") without compromising his core belief that the Bible remains the only supreme authority. He critiques the way these signatures were extracted through social pressure and haste.</meta>

# Christian Dialogue
<page-num>40</page-num>
->Christian Dialogue<-

I could well tolerate preferring people to books (as is reasonable). But had they set any other single book above the writings of the **Prophets** and **Apostles**, I would not have concurred so impulsively. Moreover, it was a hasty over-hurrying or a rushing, such that one was not granted several days or weeks to read through such things for oneself. Instead, it was read aloud to the whole crowd in only an hour, and then the <term>subscription</term> <note>The formal signing of a confession of faith, such as the Formula of Concord (1577).</note> was demanded.

Thirdly, it would not have been fitting for me, a poor listener, to prepare and set a feast of joy for the Devil, so that the whole crowd would have cried out: "There, there! We knew well that he was in agreement with our teaching." Thus, my immovable <term>Apostolic foundation</term> would have had to be held as a lying doctrine—which is not pleasing to God—to **cast pearls before swine**, or to **give what is holy to the dogs** <note>Matthew 7:6. The author suggests that explaining his true, deeper faith to a hostile or uncomprehending crowd would be a waste of sacred truth.</note>. As a reward, they would have trampled and torn me; it would have rightly happened to me that I would have shortened my life before its time. My confession would have been useful to no one in that whole crowd, only offensive; no one would have turned away from the false doctrine. I would have been harmed, and they would have been helped not at all, and many things would have remained undone through my untimely confessing.

God will surely tell me when I should speak to the <term>universities</term> <note>original: "hohen Schulen."</note>: "They do not know Christ." He who runs uncalled achieves nothing. Therefore, I have no pangs of conscience about this signing. I remain, persevere, and die on this foundation that I have told you. The world may judge me as it wishes, since I have thereby testified to the greatest freedom of the Spirit: that I can be among all <term>sects</term> <note>In this period, "sects" referred to the competing denominations: Catholic, Lutheran, and Reformed (Zwinglian/Calvinist).</note> without the **shipwreck of my faith** or the violation of my conscience. My treasure lies in the heart; no sect can take it from me, whether it be the **Pope**, **Luther**, **Zwingli**, or whoever it may be.

**CON:** Whoever believes with the heart and confesses with the mouth will be saved. <note>original: "Qui me confitetur coràm hominibus. &c." / Matthew 10:32: "Whoever confesses me before men, him I will also confess before my Father."</note>

**AUD:** To him who demands the foundation of faith for improvement, and desires to learn with a good heart, one should confess and withhold nothing.

<summary>The Auditor defends his decision to sign church documents while maintaining a private, "Apostolic" faith that transcends sectarian labels like Lutheranism or Papism. He argues against forced, public confessions that would only lead to persecution without changing hearts.</summary>

...hold. But he who says he can see, yet is actually blind and has no intention of accepting the truth, preferring to remain in his old <term>creed</term> <note>original: *Credo*. Nothing about him could comfort me if he did not dwell in me through faith. I would possess only a meager "treasure in earthly vessels" if I were to wander back and forth among the <term>sects</term> <note>Religious factions or denominations.</note> without the death of Christ being active within me.

**First**, if the killing and the crucifying of the flesh were to happen through the indwelling of Christ, then Christ’s own Cross and his death on the Cross would be of no use. 

**Secondly**, we would then have to live and walk exactly as Christ lived and walked: pure, clean, holy, righteous, and without sin. 

**Thirdly**, all people would have to fall into despair and lose heart because of their sins, since no one could kill his own flesh sufficiently, etc. 

**Fourthly**, we would not be holding Christ’s merit or his death as enough to cover the sins of the whole world. **Behold, here is the lamb who takes away the sins** <note>original: *Ecce hic est agnus, qui tollit peccata*. A reference to John 1:29.</note>...
