<margin>5.</margin> ...of the world. Fifthly, **imputed righteousness through faith** <note>original: *justitia imputativa per fidem*. A central Reformation doctrine that God "credits" Christ's righteousness to a believer's account.</note> would be abolished, and the honor due to Christ would be stripped away and instead attributed to our "killing of the flesh," as if we had to earn it ourselves.

<margin>6.</margin> Sixthly, the power and efficacy of the holy, most venerable <term>Sacraments</term> <note>Sacraments: Sacred rituals, like Baptism and Communion, believed to convey God's grace to the participant.</note> would be taken away. That is, if we were not to have, fetch, earn, and obtain the forgiveness of sins therein? This is the poor sinner's only comfort: that they draw strength from the Sacraments so that they do not despair or face damnation because of the sins they commit daily out of the weakness of their faith. For in this way, the merit of Christ is **applied** <note>original: *appliciret*</note> to them and likewise **imputed** <note>original: *imputiret*</note> through the faith they have in Christ's death. In short, if the "crucifixion and killing of the flesh" were to continue within us, then holy **Absolution** in Confession would be in vain, and we would have no comfort, nor could we have a single happy day in the world for as long as we live. Anabaptists were radical reformers who emphasized personal holiness and adult baptism; the Preacher uses this as a derogatory label for anyone demanding more than simple faith.</note> teaching!

Truly, you should think better of me; you do not know **Christ the Crucified**.

Referring to the elite theological faculties of the time.</note> and have therefore read the Scriptures more thoroughly than you?

# On True Christianity
<page-num>43</page-num>

...the world would neither know nor recognize the crucified Christ. 

**Preacher:** Be quiet; I advise you for your own good and for your welfare. You would not be tolerated in the world for saying such things. These scholars and authorities rule over all the heads in the world and are the most prominent figures at the courts of kings and princes. 

**Auditor:** Yet Herod and the scribes persecuted the child Jesus, and in truth, he was neither born nor found in Jerusalem, but rather in Ephrata, in Bethlehem <note>The Auditor is making a point about spiritual geography: the "great" centers of power (Jerusalem/scholars) often miss Christ, who is found in humble places.</note>. 

**Preacher:** You come with sharp words that do not help your case. Do you think that Emperors, Kings, Princes, and the <term>high schools</term> <note>original: *hohen Schulen*. Referring to the great universities and theological faculties of the time.</note> are without Christ? He is preached everywhere and is well enough known. 

**Auditor:** Where the life of Christ is not present within a person, there his Cross and Death are not present either. Whoever does not die with Christ—according to the meaning of **Baptism** and the **Holy Supper**—has neither seen nor recognized Christ, whether he be an Emperor, Prince, Doctor, Beggar, or Peasant. 

The death of Christ is the highest and most necessary thing in all believers. Without this death, no life is to be hoped for, neither in this world nor the next. This "life" is the renewal of the human being; it must happen within the person, where the earthly members are killed and the body is crucified with Christ along with its lusts and desires. All of this must take place and be accomplished within the person, and not merely exist outside of them, nor be <term>imputed</term> <note>original: *zugerechnet*. A central theological debate: is righteousness merely "credited" to a person's account by God (imputed), or must it actually transform their inner nature?</note> from the outside. 

Christ did not suffer for our sins so that we should continue to live in those same sins or let them rule in our mortal bodies. Rather, he suffered so that we, as new creatures, might walk according to the **new man** in Christ and not at all according to the **old man** in Adam <note>The "New Man" and "Old Adam" are Paul’s metaphors for the spiritual nature vs. the fallen, sinful nature.</note>. And although the works of the devil are destroyed within us through the indwelling of Christ—that is, our sins are subdued—this is still not our own work or merit. It is the work of Jesus Christ within us. We do not take away his honor; rather, we give him much more honor and praise his suffering and dying within us. His life is indeed revealed in our mortal bodies when we die with him. 

And even if we walk as he walked, it is still not our own merit or our own work. We are simply bound to do it out of duty and obedience, without any regard...

<sig>I ij</sig>

<summary>The Auditor argues against the Preacher's reliance on institutional authority and "external" righteousness, insisting that true Christianity requires a literal, internal death to sin and a renewal of the "inner man." He maintains that this transformation is not a "human work" but the actual life of Christ manifesting within the believer.</summary>

# A Christian Conversation
<page-num>44</page-num>

...looking toward the reward; for he rules and lives in us as in his house, dwelling, and temple. Whoever is born of God walks as Christ walked, follows in his footsteps, and remains in the Baptism he has received, thereby not denying the power of Baptism. He remains in the memory of Christ’s death and does not trample the blood of Christ underfoot. Worthily he receives the Lord’s Supper, not for the wiping away of his sins *by the work performed* <note>original: *ex opere operato*. This is a theological term meaning that a sacrament is effective simply by being performed, regardless of the recipient's inner state. The Auditor is arguing against this view.</note>. 

Sins were wiped away once and for all on the wood of the Cross; the Sacrament does not wipe them away, otherwise even the unbelievers would obtain forgiveness of sins from it. In his prayer, each person also receives absolution; he needs no "Absolver" <note>A person, such as a priest, who officially grants forgiveness.</note>, for forgiveness stands in the conscience through the Holy Spirit, and not in another’s mouth or hand. For otherwise we would have to seek it from without, here or there, and it would not be within us, yet Christ testifies and says: "The Kingdom of God is within you."

**Preacher:** With such teaching and speech of yours, you strike out all the sects in the world, implying that they cannot be in the Holy Church. 

**Auditor:** The Holy Catholic Church <note>The Auditor uses "Catholic" in its literal sense: the "universal" or "all-embracing" church of all believers, rather than the Roman Catholic institution.</note> knows of no sect nor human order; it is founded on no man, no city, land, nor specific people, but only on Jesus Christ the cornerstone. Whoever is in Christ, and Christ in him, crucifies his flesh along with its lusts and desires, and is in the Holy Church—wherever he may be, whether among the followers of Luther, the Pope, or the Turks <note>A common 16th-century term for Muslims.</note>, etc. No one may tear him from it. 

For through love he is a new creature in Christ Jesus, created for good works. But whoever will not die with Christ through Baptism and the Lord's Supper remains an old creature, *outside the true Church* <note>original: *extra veram Ecclesiam*</note>, wherever he may be. 

**Preacher:** Whoever does not hold to all points and articles with the crowd among whom he lives will be put under the ban <note>Excommunicated or legally outlawed.</note> and cast out of the church.

**PREACHER:** Since now no sect is the Church of God, where then is she to be sought, and among which people?

**AUDITOR:** Understand me correctly: I neither praise nor scold any sect (for I hope to remain a member of the true Church); I do not exalt any sect (I say), nor do I reject any. But I say this for a fundamental understanding of the true Church, that one must take note of something here, so that one might escape this error. Namely, every sect *according to its kind* <note>original Latin: *quo ad genus*</note>, as far as it concerns the crowd in general, is not the holy Church; otherwise, she would be bound to a certain external people. But *according to its individual members* <note>original Latin: *quo ad speciem*</note>, that is as far as individuals are concerned, the Church is found among all peoples, heathens, and languages. Even if I am among the Turks <note>A common 16th and 17th-century term for Muslims or those living within the Ottoman Empire.</note> according to the body, I am nevertheless in the Holy Catholic <note>Used here in the sense of the "Universal" Christian Church rather than the Roman Catholic denomination.</note> Church *according to faith and the inner man* <note>original Latin: *quo ad fidem & internum hominem*</note>; and there are also many other pious Christians among the Turks who remain members of the holy Church.

**PREACHER:** If faithful Christians were to be among the Turks, they would surely be led away from the faith and would not remain living stones in the spiritual house, as Peter speaks <note>A reference to 1 Peter 2:5: "Ye also, as lively stones, are built up a spiritual house..."</note>.

**AUDITOR:** The faith of Christ stands on no sand, but on the Rock; it remains immovable and does not let even the gates of hell prevail against it. For the *general category detracts nothing from the specific member* <note>original Latin: *genus nihil derogat speciei*</note>; the common group neither gives nor takes anything from the individual. This is to say: it does no harm to the faithful Christian that he is within a sect among the unbelievers, just as it conversely profits the unbeliever nothing toward salvation <term>salvation|German: Seligkeit; refers to the state of being saved and entering heaven</term> that he dwells among or beside pious Christians. The place does not condemn, nor does it make one blessed. What would it help a devil if he were in heaven, so to speak? Or what would it harm an angel if he were in hell, so to speak? Not at all. Everything depends on the inner foundation.

**PREACHER:** You bring forward unheard-of, strange speech, which is not customary in our writings and pulpits.

Therefore, no sect should persecute, hate, or kill another; otherwise, one would seize the little sheep along with the wolves. Whichever sect wars against or persecutes another only betrays itself, showing that it does not belong to the holy Church and is not in Christ. For by eternal truth, no Christian kills a Christian, nor even a non-Christian; no sheep goes to war, only the wolves, lions, dogs, and bears, who are outside the Church, outside faith, outside love <note>original Latin: *extra Ecclesiam, extra fidem, extra dilectionem*</note>.

**PREACHER:** It is quite true; for if one sect were to root out another, or one people were to kill another entirely, the little sheep would be seized with the wolves. # Christian Dialogue
<page-num>48</page-num>

He who is alive according to the Spirit knows Christ and is known by Christ; he is a member of the true Church of God; he is crucified to the world and the world to him in return. Let no one think that he can make Christ and the world one; a Christian has stripped off the world; he is dead to the flesh, to blood, to the world, and to the entire animal man. <note>Original Latin: *mortuus est carni, sanguini & mundo & toti homini animali*. The "animal man" refers to the natural, unspiritual state of humanity.</note>

**PREACHER:** So you summarize everything in the death of Christ? Does everything depend upon dying with Christ?

**AUDITOR:** If there were no dying or death, there could be no Christ. Everything is done for the sake of the *renovation* <note>original: *renovation* (renewal)</note>, for the sake of the renewal, the fruit, and the gain. For in death lies the fruit, as all of nature testifies. If Christ were to come into life and enter into salvation, he had to die first. If a person is to come to a new life, he must, through Christ in faith, die to the "old man." <note>A Pauline concept referring to the sinful, unregenerate nature.</note> If a grain of wheat is to bring forth fruit and have a new life, it must rot and die. If a tree or other plant is to grow forth to produce fruit, it must first die. If an ore is to become a solid metal, it must first die in the fire, be broken, and be killed. And the more often a metal "dies," the nobler it becomes by casting off its old body. Likewise, the more often a person dies, the greater the illuminations he receives, so that he passes from one degree of clarity to another—only through the death of Jesus Christ within himself, and by no means outside of himself.

**PREACHER:** You call the repentance or justification of man a "dying," as I have also mentioned before.

**AUDITOR:** Every believer or baptized person has "put on" Jesus Christ and has thereby consented and sworn to renounce three things; that is, to die to three things during his life. The first is the lust of the flesh—that is, worldly desires—according to the old man, which, if they remain in a person, bring eternal death, for they wage war against the soul. The second is the lust of the eyes or Mammon <note>A term from the New Testament referring to wealth or material greed, often personified as a false god.</note>, meaning that he must separate his heart from it, and from the love of people, whether it be father, mother, brother, sister, wife, child, friend, etc., and live in Christ Jesus alone. The...

<summary>The Auditor argues that spiritual rebirth requires a mystical "death" to the self and the world, using metaphors of seeds, plants, and refining metal to illustrate how dying leads to higher illumination. <page-num>53</page-num>
->**Concerning True Christianity**<-
G iii
<meta>catchword: and</meta>

No person would be saved, except perhaps for children who do not listen to you. For through your preaching, the listener is led away from God and His Word toward human ideas—even though you reverse it and say of those who allow themselves to be taught by God: "They lead away from the written word" <note>original Latin: *Abducunt à verbo scripto*</note>. Oh no, oh no! Those whom the Holy Spirit teaches—that is the internal **anointing** <term>Salbung: A biblical reference (1 John 2:27) to the Holy Spirit dwelling within a believer, providing direct spiritual insight without the need for human teachers.</term>—they accept the Scripture with joy and can test all preachers, spirits, and books, and be led astray by none. 

**MORS.** <note>Death.</note> I dare say that with your "mouth-preaching" <note>original: *Maulpredigen*; a derogatory term used by mystical reformers to describe clergy who spoke of God with their mouths but did not experience Him in their hearts.</note>, your writings, and your sermon books, you cause all the misery in the world, ruining land and people both temporally and eternally. You lead them all away from Jesus Christ, the only **Mediator** <term>Mittler: In Christian theology, the role of Jesus as the bridge or go-between for God and humanity.</term>, solely because you deny the internal hearing or Word—that is, you quench the Spirit in yourself and in others. 

**AVD.** <note>The Auditor or Listener.</note> Look, dear sir, at the remarkably great damage that comes from the denial of the internal hearing. For by such quenching of the Spirit in yourselves and others, you are blind, deaf, and mute, yet you wish to teach others. You truly lack the testimony of Jesus in your heart and in your life; therefore, you are blind, without light, and without life. You do not want to hear God, or what He might speak within you; you cast away the indwelling of the living Word. Therefore, you remain deaf, wishing to chatter with other people's tongues, and not to experience, feel, or taste the truth from within <note>original Latin: *ab intra*</note>. Therefore, you are mute. As you are, so you also make your listeners; if the kind and faithful God did not especially teach simple hearts through His holy inspiration, no person would be saved, even if you preached and wrote **commentaries** <note>original Latin: *Commentaria*</note> on the **Bible** <note>original Latin: *Biblia*</note> until Judgment Day. 

**MORS.** <note>Death.</note> This is the first, most prominent, and greatest deception in the world: taking away, denying, and abolishing the learning from God, the anointing, or the testimony of the Spirit. By this very act, you poke out the eyes of your listeners, robbing them of the hearing and the tongue of the internal person, making them like yourself to your eternal ruin...

<summary>In this passage, Death and the Auditor argue that academic preaching and literalist scripture study are spiritual dead ends if they lack the "internal anointing" of the Holy Spirit. They accuse the clergy of "quenching the Spirit" and leading the faithful toward spiritual blindness by focusing only on external words rather than internal experience.</summary>

# Christian Dialogue
<page-num>54</page-num>

...and damnation of body and soul. For behold—the Emperor, the King, the Prince, the Count, the Knight, the Nobleman, the Citizen, the Merchant, the City, the learned and unlearned, the rich and the poor—all are your listeners. Over these you rule within your self-appointed clerical estate; they fall down before you and say: "Our Father, who art on Earth," <note>A scathing satirical inversion of the Lord’s Prayer, suggesting that the clergy have usurped God’s place and demand the worship due to Him.</note> etc. You hold the key to heaven; open it for me; absolve me; forgive me my sins in the **Confessional** <term>Beichte: The rite of private confession to a priest to receive absolution.</term> and from the **Pulpit** <term>Cantzel: The raised platform from which the sermon is delivered.</term>. Show me the way to life; whatever you teach, I will follow as if God spoke it from heaven. Thus, you are a people without a head, for you rule over the Emperor, King, Princes, Lords, Counts, Barons, and over the rich and poor. No one is above you; you set yourself in God’s place as the **Vicar of Christ** <note>original Latin: *Vicarius Christi*; historically the title of the Pope, here used to critique any minister who claims absolute divine authority.</note>. 

On the other hand, your listener is your head and lord according to physical power—a situation which you justly bring upon yourself through your false teaching. Therefore, the Emperor, the King, the Prince, the Count, the Nobleman, or the City takes you, drives you out, chases you away, throws you into prison, and lets you rot and perish there. Even though you were previously a spiritual father and a good advisor to them, this happens to you justly at the hands of your listener, whose eyes you have gouged out. By denying the **internal Word** and by **quenching the Spirit**, you have led them toward human books (which I will not list, though your listener has often named them to you). I say you have led them toward human writings and not toward the **anointing** <term>Salbung: Direct spiritual insight or "unction" from the Holy Spirit (1 John 2:27), which Radical Reformers argued made human teachers unnecessary.</term> in their own hearts. Because of this, the listener is blind and can test neither you nor others to see if you are right. 

But so that your rule over the **potentates** <term>Potentaten: Monarchs or powerful political rulers.</term> and heads of the world does not diminish or depart—even if you are exiled or imprisoned—your cohorts are there. They also stand against you before princes and lords until the same thing happens to them at the hands of the powerful as what you suffer now. Thus, one must always be against the other in such blindness: the **secular** <term>Weltliche: Civil authorities or laypeople, as opposed to the church hierarchy.</term> against the **clerical** <term>Geistlichen: Members of the ministry or church leadership.</term>, and both of these against the subject, because you [quench] the light or the spirit in yourself and in 

<meta>catchword: others (German: *andern*)</meta>

<summary>The Auditor argues that by denying the internal Holy Spirit, the clergy have created a cycle of mutual blindness and tyranny where they usurp God's authority over the state, only to be justly persecuted by the very rulers they misled.</summary>

# On True Christianity

**AUDITOR:** O Death, how truly you speak! The Spirit would make preachers and listeners one—one spirit in faith, one baptism through love in Jesus Christ. But since such a treasure has been taken out of the heart through your teaching, shouting, and preaching without the Spirit, all blind listeners must live in fear of heresy. They hold lies for truth and Christ for the Devil; even if God sends one of His truly learned ones, you and your listeners do not recognize him. You are consumed by a fear that you might be led astray. For if you were built upon the rock of Jesus Christ through the **testimony of Jesus** <term>Zeugnus Jesu: The internal confirmation of faith within a believer's heart.</term> in the heart, who could possibly lead you into error or deceive you?

**DEATH:** Every sinner punishes himself, and by whatever a man sins, by that he is plagued and tormented. For behold, you preacher: you have quenched the Spirit and the inner Word until it even reeks in the nostrils of your listeners—that is, before kings, princes, lords, counts, cities, and merchants. Therefore, you and your listeners are justly deprived of the testimony of Jesus in the heart—which is the Light—and you must instead see only what others have seen, hear with foreign ears, and taste with another’s tongue.

**AUDITOR:** Yes, indeed, it is a miserable, pitiful thing that we could have eyes of our own, yet we willfully allow them to be put out. We believe on mere persuasion whatever our preacher writes, says, and presents. Which prince or king is there who does not cling to his self-appointed prophets or preachers and rest upon what they have written and spoken? Which count, knight, or nobleman does not have his own particular **pastor** <term>Seelsorger: Literally a "soul-carer"; a personal chaplain or local minister.</term>? Which city is there that does not keep its own special teachers, and lets stand whatever comes from

# A Christian Dialogue
<page-num>56</page-num>

...said from the pulpit? All the world gapes at the outward babbling of the mouth; no one enters into God, into the school of Jesus Christ. No one asks for the anointing, for the Word in the heart and mouth of the **inner man** <note>The spiritual part of a person that communicates directly with God, as opposed to the "outer man" who relies on physical senses and ceremonies.</note>, through which one should examine oneself and test all spirits to see if they were of God.

**DEATH:** You lying tongue! You could not have made it worse (it is to you **preachers** <term>Predicanten: A term often used for Protestant ministers, sometimes pejoratively in this context to imply they only talk without spiritual power.</term> I speak) than by labeling as "imperial" or "fanatical" the anointing of the Spirit, the drawing of the Father, and so on. For in this way, you have made yourself lord and head over the worldly sword. You have placed yourself above all **potentates** <term>Potentaten: Rulers, monarchs, or those holding great political power.</term> and heads of the world, so that they must all fall down before you and worship you: emperors, kings, princes, lords, knights, counts, noble and commoner, merchants, high and low, rich and poor. All of these come to them and seek **absolution** <note>The formal release from guilt or sin pronounced by a priest or minister.</note>, etc. For you have deprived them of the best part; they are blind, they confess, but they know nothing of holy prayer, or of the **third article of sanctification** <note>A reference to the section of the Christian Creed concerning the Holy Spirit, which the author argues should empower the individual believer rather than the church hierarchy.</note>—namely, that every believing, baptized Christian in the New Testament should and must be his own priest and **absolver** <note>One who grants forgiveness of sins; the author argues for the "priesthood of all believers."</note> in Christ Jesus. A blind man is easily led wherever one wishes. 

**AUDITOR:** No prince, emperor, king, or regent wishes to be without your Dignity. All follow you in counsel, governance, and life, etc. No one wants to sit down with King David and hear what the Lord says within him. No one wants to ask God for counsel; no one wants to obtain forgiveness of sins without confessing before you, without your **absolution**. No one will accept a book without your permission. So great is your **authority** and reputation, so powerful is your dominion over all estates, potentates, and heads of the world. You have brought this about by abandoning and denying the **inner Word** <term>innern Worts: The mystical or spiritualist concept that God’s true Word is spoken directly to the soul by the Holy Spirit, rather than just being found in the written text of the Bible.</term>, so that everyone must believe you on your smooth talk alone. You have so thoroughly enchanted all people and seized and possessed all lordships that they must be blind and see with foreign eyes—that is, they must see as you see—and they must chatter with other people's tongues, that is, as your Dignity...

## Of True Christianity

...taste; so must it also taste to them. Is that not a great use of force, that all people are so willing to let their eyes be gouged out and to see with foreign eyes?

**D E A T H:** You **Preacher** <term>Concionator: Latin for a preacher or public speaker; here it carries a critical tone toward clergy who focus on rhetoric over spirit.</term>, your power is great, which you have brought about through your false tongues. But my power is even greater. You are like a people without a head; no prince nor Emperor may dispense with you. They are all under you and fall down before you. If they wish to hang a thief or kill a sinner, they must ask you for advice, for they have not learned enough from **Christ’s Law** <note>original: "Christi Gesetz"; the moral and spiritual teachings of Jesus, which the author argues should be the primary guide for justice rather than clerical dictates.</note> to know what should be done. They see with your eyes and all fall down before you. 

But you and they all must fall down before me, whether it brings you joy or sorrow; no one may escape my power. I have received this power from Him who sent me into the whole world: Jesus Christ, who says of Himself, "I am **God’s Word** <note>original: "Gottes Wort"</note>, a King of all kings and a Lord of all lords; to me all power is given in heaven and on earth; I am the Alpha and the Omega, the Beginning and the End." 

Now, just as my Lord Christ (whose name you carry in your mouth, but whose power you deny) has all power in heaven and earth, so I also have all power over all people who live under the sun. I rule over everything that lives within the **circle of the firmament** <term>Firmament: The heavens or the sky, traditionally viewed as a vast dome or sphere surrounding the earth.</term>. I am everywhere: in the earth, in the sea, with all lords, servants, the rich, the poor, the learned, the unlearned, the high, and the lowly. All **potentates** <term>Potentaten: Rulers, monarchs, or those holding supreme political power.</term> and all heads of the world must tremble and quake before my power, strength, and might. 

Indeed, all the brave soldiers have not escaped my hands. When I step into the Emperor’s chamber or the King’s palace, his heart turns cold. When I come to the prince who has persecuted and killed so many people for the sake of the **faith** <note>original: "Glaubens willen"</note>, he must tremble and be terrified before me. When I come to the Count who has strangled and hanged so many people for the sake of animals and fish <note>A reference to the brutal "Forest Laws" of the era, where peasants were often executed for poaching game or fishing in aristocratic waters.</note>, I lead him straightway out into the **terrible darkness** <note>original: "schreckliche Finsternuß"; often a metaphor for death or divine judgment.</note>. This happens to him because he followed you, saw with your eyes, and believed he was doing what would please...

# Christian Dialogue
<page-num>58</page-num>

...pleased, and burned himself on the one who should be within him, namely Christ my Lord. Those who in this time have loved the testimony of Jesus and walked in the light, pass through you into rest and into the **Eternal Light** <note>original: "Ewige Liecht"</note>. But those who have smothered and denied the inner testimony, the light in the heart, and the Spirit, you send into unrest and into eternal darkness. There they shall become aware of why they contended over faith on earth and persecuted others, whom they nonetheless never possessed, and why they defended the **Word of God** with war—which they neither had in their hearts nor wished to hear. Who would fear you, who lets Christ, the Word of the Father, speak in their heart? Christ is in us and you are in us all, and you shall become but a sweet sleep.

**DEATH:** He who fears me has never yet seen, recognized, nor tasted Christ through faith; for I and Christ are one. But he who fears me does not have Christ within him. He who fears **heretics or sects** <note>original: "Ketzern oder Secten"</note> and for that reason forbids and hinders printing—because he feels that his house, built upon sand, might be overturned by storm winds and floods—he is not one of Christ’s little sheep, cannot taste the pasture, and must fear all books and writings. But for the faithful, newborn person who has Christ within him as light and life, all heresy must serve to sharpen, awaken, and improve him; it is impossible that he should be torn down or led astray by a false book or preacher.

### On True Christianity
<page-num>59</page-num>

...is afraid of sects and heretics, and out of the good intention of his own mind—so that his subjects might not be led away from the faith—such a thing happens at the prompting of the **preacher** <term>concionator: A Latin term for a preacher or public speaker, often used here to refer to the established clergy who influenced the nobility.</term>. He who does not have the touchstone <note>original: "Prüfestein"; a stone used to test the purity of gold, here referring to internal spiritual discernment.</note> within himself, so too the **listener** <term>auditor: One who hears the word; in this context, the common layperson or subject.</term> is also blind; so that out of ignorance he forbids printing in his land and must see through the eyes of the preacher. If faith, or Christ, or the **Anointing** <note>original: "Salbung"; likely a reference to the "anointing from the Holy One" in 1 John 2:20, implying direct spiritual knowledge.</note> were in both of these, they could fear no sects, nor would they ever forbid printing. For what is false does the believer no harm; it awakens and sharpens him toward the good. What is righteous does the wicked no good, but it strengthens the simple-hearted.

**AUDITOR:** I conclude for myself that this is so; God through His mercy has given the Light into my heart—Jesus Christ the bright morning star—who teaches me from within through His Word or His inspiration <note>original: "einsprechen"; literally "in-speaking" or divine internal address.</note>, more than all books and preachers could teach me until the Last Day. Thus I see with my own eyes, through God, and cannot be led astray by writings, books, or preachers; I read and examine all sects and books in the whole world. Oh, that princes and **potentates** <term>potentaten: Powerful rulers or monarchs.</term> could see such things with their own eyes! Never again would they need to fear sects, nor could they forbid printing in their lands.

**DEATH:** Your wish is in vain. If they were to see with their own eyes, they would have to follow the drawing of the Father and allow themselves to be taught by the Anointing; then they would become more learned than the preacher himself and see through the deception. Whereupon the preacher, through writings and sermons, would proclaim the King or Lord to have become a **follower of Müntzer, or Schwenkfeld, an Enthusiast, an Anabaptist, or a Sacramentarian** <note>These were various labels—Müntzerisch, Schwenckfeldisch, Enthusiastisch, Widertäufferisch, Sacramentirisch—used by the established church to brand dissenters as radicals or dangerous heretics.</note>. Now, no one wishes to be mocked, scorned, slandered, and have their name brought to shame for Christ’s sake. It is enough that Christ Himself suffered and did such things for the world; they think they need not suffer it. Therefore, your wish is in vain and for nothing.

<summary>In this passage, Death and the Auditor argue that true spiritual "Anointing" removes the fear of heresy and the need for book censorship. Death cynically observes that rulers remain "blind" and dependent on clergy because they fear the social shame of being labeled as radicals or heretics.</summary>

# Christian Dialogue / The Fifth Chapter. #

## Here again Death, the Preacher, and the Listener speak with one another, and it is proven that the necessary essential indwelling of God in man is not heretical or Osiandrian, but Apostolic and Prophetic. ##

**DEATH.** Since you, **Preacher** <term>concionator: A formal term for a public preacher, often used here to describe state-appointed clergy who influence the decisions of rulers.</term>, have rejected the internal testimony, the anointing, or the "drawing of the Father" as fanatical—both in speech and in writing—you can now easily lead your listener (who follows you) away from the eternal marriage and covenant between God and man, where an essential, physical indwelling must remain for eternity.

**LISTENER.** [The Preacher] would likely not admit to me that we must be in Christ and Christ must be in us according to an essential, physical union. Instead, he slanders me as an **Osiandrian** <note>A follower of Andreas Osiander (1498–1552), a reformer who taught that righteousness comes from Christ's divine nature actually dwelling within the believer, rather than just being a legal "credit" from God. This was a highly controversial view at the time.</note>, even though I have learned this not from Osiander, but from Christ and Paul.

**DEATH.** So it goes: you do not enter heaven yourself, and you wish to shut it against your listener as well. Whoever is outside of Christ is not in Love, not in Faith, and not in God; such a person cannot enter heaven.

**PREACHER.** If a person were not made righteous and saved by something other than the essential, physical indwelling of Christ, then our faith would be false and all our comfort would be gone. Three reasons move me not to accept such Osiandrian teaching. 

First, even the damned, the unbelievers, and even the devils have God within them, and they cannot be "outside" of Christ, in whom all things move, stand, live, and float <note>A reference to Acts 17:28, "For in him we live and move and have our being."</note>; and yet, they are not made righteous and saved by this. That is to say: Nature does not save; only Grace does. 

Second, the grace of God would be cast aside and the merit of Christ would be nullified, as if his sacrifice were not enough or powerful enough for the justification of a person from all their sins. In that case, faith would be nothing—the faith that grasps the merit of Christ, which is where we should find our comfort. Indeed, all people would have to despair without **imputed righteousness** <note>original: *justitiam imputativam*; the theological concept that God "credits" Christ's righteousness to a sinner's account, even if the sinner remains inherently flawed.</note>, which my Listener wants to abolish. As for the

<meta>catchword: third/</meta>

<summary>This page presents a theological debate regarding "essential indwelling"—the idea that Christ physically lives within a believer—contrasted against the orthodox Lutheran view of "imputed righteousness." The Preacher argues that if God's presence alone saved a person, then even the damned would be saved, as God is omnipresent in all of nature.</summary>

# on true Christianity. #

->G iii<-

...thirdly, I do not accept it because such teaching is publicly rejected in cities and lands, in schools and churches; how would I dare now to profess it in high schools or preach it from the pulpit? Would I not be most shamefully slandered, suppressed, and even driven out of the country?

**DEATH.** You do not know Christ; you do not possess His teaching or His life—He who was branded a heretic and condemned by the world because He could not conform to it. Whoever condemns such teaching sins against the Holy Spirit, and it will be forgiven him neither in this world nor in the next.

**LISTENER.** We must not accept and believe a thing simply because many people in the world believe it, but rather what the few hold for truth, and what the Holy Spirit testifies in our hearts through the anointing <note>original: "Salbung" — refers to the "unction" or spiritual insight granted to believers mentioned in 1 John 2:27</note>. Whoever is not willing to be despised, mocked, shamed, and called a heretic with Christ on earth should not bother becoming a Christian.

**DEATH.** You bring three causes, **Preacher** <term>concionator: A public preacher or orator.</term>, why you cannot accept or recognize the teaching of Christ (that is, the Prophetic and Apostolic foundation of the essential indwelling of God in man). But they are all insufficient reasons to abandon the truth or to deny Christ's life. For what of the argument that all the damned and unbelieving (and even the devils) are in God and cannot be outside of Him, yet they are not saved by it—and therefore the believing reborn cannot become righteous or saved through such an essential indwelling?

**LISTENER.** To one who does not have the inner testimony of the Spirit in his heart, that might seem a sufficiently weighty reason. For the Spirit of the Lord fills all things, is in all, and through all; the Word is the light and life in all creatures. The light shines in the darkness—that is, in the rational creature—and the darkness cannot comprehend it, and yet no one is saved by it except the **Reborn** <term>Renatus: Latin for "one who is born again," referring to the spiritual rebirth of a believer.</term> or the believer in Christ.

<margin>Galatians 6</margin>

**DEATH.** Take note: all creatures live in the Word or the Spirit, but they do not all walk in the Spirit; as the Apostle testifies: "If we live in the Spirit, let us [walk in the Spirit]." <note>Death is quoting Galatians 5:25, though the margin notes Galatians 6, likely referring to the broader context of the "new creature."</note>

# Christian Dialogue #

...and also walk in the Spirit. That is, only the believers walk in that in which they live. It is not enough that God and His Word are in us; we must also be in God and in His Word. It is not enough that heaven and the kingdom of God be in humans; humans must also be in heaven, or in the kingdom of God, and walk there, which happens through faith in Christ.

**LISTENER.** That God is in all of us, and His kingdom and His Word, Spirit, Law, and Heaven are in us—that is a matter of nature and from the first creation. But that we, in turn, are in God, just as He is in us—dwelling in His Word, Spirit, Law, and Kingdom—that is faith in Christ. It comes from the new creation and is called grace. Through this we are saved and made righteous, and not by nature from our first birth.

**DEATH.** You speak rightly; it is thus that nature does not save, but grace. God loves all people, even His enemies. He has poured out love into hearts through the Holy Spirit, whom He has given to all people. He loves them all; He knows them all—even the damned. However, it helps no one unless he, in turn, loves and knows God, just as he is loved and known by God. From this, you see that everything is already within the human being: the anointing, the love, and the Word; it only must be learned, found, recognized, and tasted.

**LISTENER.** The inner testimony, the anointing in everyone <note>original: "in quolibet" — in each person or in every individual</note>, is indeed undeniable; yet it is shamefully cried out from your pulpits as a delusion and fanaticism <note>original: "Schwermerey" — a derogatory term used by orthodox reformers to describe those who claimed direct private revelation from the Holy Spirit</note>.

**DEATH.** That the Preacher <term>Concionator: a public preacher or orator</term> is himself a fanatic and a seducer has been sufficiently proven above. For now, you should remember that the reasons brought forward by the Preacher amount to nothing at all and are of no use. 

Do you see it, **Preacher**? You cannot stand with these reasons: that you know God is in you, and that the light shines in you, yet you do not also wish to be a light in God through the new birth.

**LISTENER.** So the second reason will be just as weak as the first, where he teaches me—with both mouth and pen—that faith, the grace of God, and the merit of Christ would be diminished and cast aside if all...

It refutes the idea that "essential indwelling" is a heretical delusion, arguing instead that salvation requires a reciprocal relationship of love and recognition between God and man.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Death (Mors), the Listener (Auditor), and the Preacher (Concionator). The discussion has moved from the nature of God's presence to a specific theological debate: whether salvation comes from Christ's righteousness being "credited" to a person (imputed righteousness) or from Christ actually living within the person (essential indwelling).</meta>

# On True Christianity #

...people would have to despair if we were to become righteous and blessed only through the **essential indwelling** of Christ in us, and through nothing from **imputed righteousness** <note>original: "imputatitiam justitiam" — the theological doctrine that God "credits" Christ's perfection to a sinner's account, rather than the person actually becoming perfect themselves</note>, or through Holy Baptism and the Sacrament. 

**DEATH.** <note>original: "MORS"</note> I ask you, **Preacher** <note>original: "Concionator"</note>, tell me: to whom is the merit, suffering, and death of Christ credited? Is it not so: only to the believer or those baptized through the Holy Spirit and water? You must rightly say yes. But who is a believer through Baptism and the Lord's Supper except for a **reborn person** <note>original: "Renatus"</note>—that is, one who is born again from God? Now it is undeniable that the reborn remains in that from which he is born, and the lover remains in the beloved. **The one who loves lives essentially with the beloved** <note>original: "Diligens uiuitur cum dilecto essentialiter"</note>. 

Just as a branch remains essentially and physically united in the grapevine, how could faith now be denied since Christ **dwelling within** <note>original: "inhabitans"</note> has Himself become the faith? How could grace be denied since grace rules and dwells physically within the human being? How could a person despair and be without comfort since he is baptized through faith and Baptism into Christ's death, suffering, and dying, and has "put on" the new man, Jesus Christ, by stripping away and killing the "old man"? O, you blind leader of all the blind, how you lead yourself and others who follow you astray!

**LISTENER.** <note>original: "A V D." for Auditor</note> I do not reject **imputed righteousness** <note>original: "imputativam justitiam"</note>, for otherwise I would cast away grace—which is Christ. Rather, the suffering, death, or merit of Christ is credited to me out of grace in that I allow Christ to live, govern, and rule within me through faith. Thus, His death is the life within me, and is not merely credited beside me or outside of me **without the essential indwelling** <note>original: "sine essentiali inhabitatione"</note>. 

**DEATH.** Do you hear what the **Listener** <note>original: "Auditor"</note> says and confesses? He remains steadfast in this until his end and is not ashamed to be slandered by the world for Christ's sake—unlike you, who fear for your good name and your life, which you must lose eventually anyway. Therefore, you are not worthy to be a disciple of Christ, since you will not suffer with Him and will not allow yourself to be despised for the sake of the truth.

<summary>Death and the Listener argue that "imputed righteousness" (salvation as a legal credit) is inseparable from "essential indwelling" (Christ actually living within the believer). Death rebukes the Preacher for prioritizing his reputation over this transformative spiritual truth.</summary>

<keywords>imputed righteousness, essential indwelling, rebirth, baptism, union with Christ, spiritual suffering, radical discipleship</keywords>

# Christian Conversation

...still allow yourself to be led astray. 

**AUDITOR.** You are a Doctor of Scripture; you should rightly have read the testimonies of the prophets and apostles as well as I—and even much better—wherein we find that <term>mutual indwelling</term> <note>original: "mutua inhabitatio"</note>, that is, the **essential union** of God and man, is necessary for eternal life. It is testified by the Article of Creation, of Redemption, and of Sanctification: that we must be the City of God, the New Jerusalem from heaven. For we humans were created for this very reason: that we should be in God, and He in us; we were created in His image. 

Now, the image is not the thing itself, but belongs to that from which it comes and from which it originates. And just as little as we can exist without God, just as little does God wish to be without us in eternal linkage and union—just as Eve came from Adam, and Adam was in her, and she was in him, one flesh. Thus we are from God and remain in God, and God in us—whether it be in <term>yieldedness</term> <note>original: "Gelassenheit"; a central term in German mysticism referring to the state of letting go of self-will to be fully receptive to God</note> or faith for eternal blessed life, or in self-will and unbelief for eternal damnation. 

**DEATH.** You can prove this not only from the first creation, but also after the Fall through the <term>restoration</term> <note>original: "Widerbringung"; the theological concept of the "restitution of all things" or the return to a state of grace</note> in Christ. There, the believers are born again from God Himself, with heavenly flesh and blood. They eat His flesh and drink His blood through faith, and also in the Lord’s Supper as a remembrance. He who sent me above all men, Jesus Christ, has purchased His own from the earth through His blood, so that they are His own property, and in His kingdom they live under Him—that is, that they walk in Him as **new creatures**, and He in them. 

**AUDITOR.** Yes, we have the Spirit from the Father, and we have the body from the Son; we are of His flesh and of His bones. We carry His suffering and His dying in our mortal body, so that His life may also be revealed in us, and we are essentially united with Him through faith or Baptism, so that neither His Spirit nor His body can be separated from us for all eternity. 

**DEATH.** You have a good understanding...

# On True Christianity

understanding; you did not learn it from preachers. The **Holy Spirit**, that is, the Anointing, makes simple hearts more learned than all their teachers; and I say: many more people would be saved and enlightened on the path of God if there were not preachers who, with their shouting, preoccupy and possess the listener worse than the evil spirit.

**AUDITOR**: I also often say that many a good heart, drawn by the Father and taught, would come to Christ if the external babbling from the pulpit did not throw up a hindrance. But when I say such things, they immediately say I want to reject the *vocal word* <note>original: *verbum vocale*; the spoken word or preaching.</note> and lead away from the *written word* <note>original: *verbo scripto*; the Holy Scriptures</note> toward <term>Müntzerian</term> <note>referring to Thomas Müntzer (1489–1525), a radical reformer who emphasized direct revelation.</note> or <term>Schwenckfeldian</term> <note>referring to Caspar Schwenckfeld (1489–1561), who prioritized the "Inner Word" of the Spirit over external rituals.</note> illumination, toward the *ecstasy of the Anabaptists* <note>original: *raptus Anabaptistarum*; a derogatory reference to the radical reformers' claims of spiritual transport or direct inspiration.</note>, and become an <term>Enthusiast</term> <note>original: *Enthusiasta*; literally "one possessed by a god," used as a slur for those who claimed the Spirit spoke to them apart from the Bible.</note>. Oh Truth, I complain to you: violence is done to me! I join the internal and external together; I test the spirits through the Anointing and recognize that there are more false preachers in the world than righteous ones. Indeed, O dear Death, I would not let such things about the inner Word and hearing cross my lips if you did not stand by me as a powerful lord over the *Preacher and the Listener* <note>original: *Concionatorem & Auditorem*</note>—that is, over both estates, spiritual and worldly.

**DEATH**: Justly I am your shield and comfort, since by the command of my Lord I take away both the *Preacher* <note>original: *Concionatorem*</note> who teaches falsely and the *Listener* <note>original: *Auditorem*</note> who defends such lies, and lead them together into unrest and darkness—and even some into eternal damnation, those who remain and persist in sins against the **Holy Spirit**.

**AUDITOR**: According to my simple understanding, I consider that the *Preacher* <note>original: *Concionator*</note> entirely rejects the third article of <term>Sanctification</term> <note>the theological concept of being made holy by the Holy Spirit.</note> or <term>Illumination</term> <note>spiritual enlightenment.</note> and sins against the **Holy Spirit**. For in that he abolishes the Anointing—the inner armory <note>original: *Zeughuß*; a storehouse or arsenal, here used metaphorically for the spiritual strength within the heart.</note> in the heart—and wants to make people saved through external hearing alone, without the internal; yes, even through confessing, absolving, and taking the Sacrament. Thus the **Holy Catholic Church** <note>referring to the "Universal" or "Whole" Church of all believers, rather than the specific Roman denomination.</note> is held to be an external visible assembly, bound to a certain land and city, to a certain place and people, from which then war and murder arise.

# On True Christianity #

[...If you hold to the teaching] of our teachers and instructors <note>original Latin: *præceptorum*</note>, then you will remain safe from such fanaticism <note>original: *schwermerey*—a term often used by established reformers to dismiss radical or mystical sects as "fanatics"</note>. **THE LISTENER.** Oh, my dear sir, no; here the letter and the Spirit are one and the same thing. What the Spirit says inwardly, the letter drinks in through the senses; here the understanding is in no way one thing and the letter another. To be baptized into the death and for the death of Christ means, and indeed is: to be killed with Him, to be crucified with Him, to be buried with Him. You must necessarily connect the preposition <note>the Latin prefix "con-" or German "mit," meaning "with"</note> with the verb and speak from the bottom of your heart with the Apostle: to be born with God <note>original: *connasci ex Deo*</note>, to be baptized with Him <note>original: *conbaptizari*</note>, to dine with Him <note>original: *concœnare*</note>, to drink the cup with Him <note>original: *combibere calicem*</note>, to be buried with Him <note>original: *consepeliri*</note>, to be crucified with Him <note>original: *concrucifigi*</note>, to suffer with Him <note>original: *compati*</note>, to die with Him <note>original: *commori*</note>, etc. Then there follows also the rising with Him <note>original: *consurgere*</note> and the ascending with Him to the heavens <note>original: *coascendere ad cœlos*</note>. You are a Doctor; you understand Latin quite well; if this preposition <note>the "with"</note> were taken away, our Faith, Baptism, and the Lord’s Supper would be nothing. The death of Christ would be in vain and lost; no imputed righteousness from without <note>original: *imputativa justititia ab extra*—the legal doctrine that God simply "credits" righteousness to a person's account</note> would help there. **DEATH.** I say to you, Preacher <note>original: *Concionator*</note>, if you are not essentially and bodily united with me, according to the meaning of Baptism and the Lord's Supper, then you have Christ only halfway, and you cannot fully preach Christ the crucified; nor will you be saved with Him. For just as I was essentially and bodily one with my Lord—who sent me—upon the tree of the Cross, and it was not merely credited to Him from the outside, even so you must be bodily united with me and with Christ. Either for eternal salvation, if you believe willingly, or for eternal damnation, if you persist in unbelief. I am the door to that world; Christ accepts no one unless they have entered through me. **THE LISTENER.** You can certainly see for yourselves through a crude example that "imputation from without" is of no use without the bodily, essential union—just as food placed far away from a hungry man who is lying in chains would not satisfy him by being "imputed from the outside" <note>original: *ab extra*</note>; that is, credited to him from a distance. He must take it into himself, so that the food becomes flesh and blood in him, and unites bodily and essentially with the one eating. Likewise, Christ is the bread from heaven; He must be eaten; His flesh and blood must [be] in us

K ij

<meta>catchword: in us</meta>

<summary>The Listener and Death argue that salvation requires a literal, internal "dying with Christ" and "essential union" rather than a merely legalistic or external "imputed" righteousness. They use the analogy of eating food to explain that Christ must be physically and spiritually absorbed to be effective.</summary>

# Christian Dialogue #

[...Christ] must be in us; His death, His cross, and His suffering must happen in our own bodies, and not merely be imputed <note>original: *imputiert*—a theological term meaning to legally credit someone with a status (like "righteous") that they do not inherently possess</note> to us from the outside. "Whoever does not eat my flesh and drink my blood has no life in him" <note>referencing John 6:53</note>. And even if one wanted to excuse themselves in the **Calvinist manner** <note>Calvinists held a "spiritual" view of the Eucharist, where Christ is present in power but his physical body remains in heaven; the speaker here argues for a more literal, internal "incarnation" of the Word</note>, claiming it was only spiritual food for the soul, the Word must nevertheless become incarnate flesh within us. We must be essentially born of God, not merely in the manner of shadows. For this very reason, and for important causes, He ordained and instituted His body and blood to be eaten and drunk in bread and wine in memory of His death.

**THE PREACHER.** But how is it possible that a human being should walk as Christ walked—without sin, righteous, chaste, and pure? The **Cathars** <note>original: *Catari*—a medieval sect often accused of "perfectionism," the belief that Christians can reach a state of total sinlessness. The Preacher uses this as a derogatory label for the Listener's views</note> taught in this way.

**THE LISTENER.** Not the Cathars, but **Paul** and all the **Apostles** teach this. If we die with Christ through **Baptism**, then it is also easy for us to live with Him—that is, to walk as He walked, for His commandments are not burdensome <note>referencing 1 John 5:3</note>. Whoever says it is impossible to walk as Christ walked speaks against the very foundation of Scripture and against the Faith; for to the believer, all things are easy and possible, as He makes a **new creature**. And this new creature walks in the law of Christ quite easily. Indeed, it would be impossible for a newborn Christian *not* to keep the commandments of Christ—namely, **Love**—and through love to keep and fulfill all the commandments.

Everything depends upon this death; without death, you cannot have Christ. Whoever does not know this death does not know Christ. Whoever does not have the crucified Christ within him does not have Christ's life within him. But whoever has this death within him remains in **GOD**, from whom he is born; he also remains in Christ and walks just as He walked. If we do not suffer and die with Christ here on earth, then we may not eat and inherit at His table in **Heaven** above.

**DEATH.** <note>labeled "MORS" in the text; this may be a personification or a specific interlocutor representing the "old man" or a legalistic opponent</note> This is a terrible error in you, that you decry the writings of the Prophets and Apostles with shameful names of vice throughout all lands with mouth and pen, and say: "From God, through the **Anointing**..."

<summary>The Listener argues that true Christianity requires an internal, physical-spiritual union with Christ's suffering and death, rejecting the idea of purely "imputed" righteousness. He claims that for a "new creature" born of God, following Christ's example of sinless love is not only possible but natural.</summary>

# On True Christianity #

<margin>THE LISTENER</margin>
To be taught by the **Anointing** <note>original: *Salbung*—referring to 1 John 2:27, the idea that the Holy Spirit teaches the believer directly</note> is, according to you, to be "Schwenckfeldian" or "Münzerite" <note>Caspar Schwenckfeld and Thomas Müntzer were radical reformers often used as "bogeymen" by the orthodox to label anyone who emphasized inner spirit over external scripture or ritual</note>. You mockingly call those learned in God "Heavenly Prophets," yet you yourself remain a hellish, satanic prophet. You call the killing and **crucifixion of the flesh** <note>The ascetic practice of subduing worldly desires</note> "Monkish" and "Anabaptist." The **indwelling of God** in man you call "Osiandrian" <note>named after Andreas Osiander, who taught that Christ’s divine nature literally dwells within the believer to make them righteous, rather than just being a legal status</note>. If you sinned against a man, or against the Father and Son, it could be forgiven; but because you sin against the Holy Spirit, it can never be forgiven you. For whatever the holy Prophets, the Apostles, and Christ have spoken and written—that is indeed the Holy Spirit. Now, Christ and the Holy Spirit have said: all believers must die with Him and be crucified with Him. Yet you come here out of the **High Schools** <note>The universities</note>, and you abolish Baptism and the Last Supper according to their inner power and effect in the internal being. Instead, you dispute heatedly over the **Sacraments**—how one should extract salvation from them—while calling others "enthusiasts," "sacramentarians," and "desecrators of the sacraments."

<margin>DEATH</margin> <note>original: *MORS*—This character likely represents the "Old Man" or the "Mortal Self" undergoing the process of dying to the world, a common theme in Arndt’s mystical theology</note>
It is certainly true that when a simple listener hears such slanderous names, he is immediately terrified by them and thinks the Holy Scripture has been misunderstood by those people who are so shamefully decried before the world. Though I neither praise nor scold anyone, for it is not my place to judge. I cling to no man, let him be called what he will—be it **Luther**, or the **Pope**, **Zwingli** or **Müntzer**, **Schwenckfeld**, **Muhammad**, etc. I remain in Christ alone, in whom is gathered the entirety of the Prophetic and Apostolic Scriptures.

<margin>THE LISTENER</margin>
You do right that you cling to no man nor book. You have the **testimony of Jesus** within you. Do this: do not let yourself be driven away or killed for the sake of this or that person’s teaching, as many do now. They lack the testimony of the Spirit, so they allow themselves to be driven here and there, or even killed, for the sake of some self-appointed teacher.

No one shall be worth so much to me, nor be so highly esteemed, that I would allow myself to be killed or driven away for the sake of his writings. It is enough for me in...

# A Christian Dialogue #

To stay with Christ and to die with Him, I need not hang upon any book or man. **DEATH.** That such killing, overturning, and chasing away happens comes only from the **Preacher** <note>original: *Concionatore*</note>, who rejects the inner testimony and does not preach Christ the crucified. Because of this, the **Listener** <note>original: *Auditor*</note> becomes so blinded, so mad and insane, that even under the name of Christ he dares to chase away and kill Christ himself. I have heard no **Preacher** <note>original: *Prædicanten*</note> of this current time in **Germany** who preached Christ the crucified from the pulpit; only halfway, and from the outside, following the bare **history** <note>original: *Historia*—referring here to a mere intellectual or factual account of Jesus without its spiritual application</note>. I travel through all villages, cities, lands, and kingdoms of the world; they are all well known to me, even if they refuse to know me.

**THE LISTENER.** Although this speech is contrary to all the understanding of the world-scholars and the common man, it is nevertheless true and certain for the very reason that Christ's Gospel is a pure **paradox** <note>original: *paradoxon*</note> against all human reason. For no one wants to consider that GOD and the Devil, Christ and the world, are directly against one another. They all think Christ's teaching, life, and conduct are separate from the Gospel, or that faith is separate from Christ's life, and that one can well have and keep Christ and His life even if one remains without remorse, repentance, conversion, and the improvement of one's life. That no **Preacher** of this time preaches **Christ crucified** <note>original: *Christum crucifixum*</note> from the **pulpit** <note>original: *suggestu*</note> shall be testified and proven by me through four strong reasons. First, they cannot preach the crucified Christ because none among them knows what the **Gospel** <note>original: *Evangelium*</note> is. They think that it is called "Gospel" if they have learned a **definition** <note>original: *definitionem*</note> from the **Examination of Candidates for Ordination** <note>original: *examine ordinandorum*—the standard testing of new ministers</note>, or from the **Body of Doctrine** <note>original: *corpore doctrinæ*—the collection of foundational church teachings</note>, or from the **Augsburg Confession** <note>original: *Augustana Confessione*—the primary statement of faith for the Lutheran Church, presented in 1530</note>, or from the **Church Fathers** <note>original: *Patribus*</note>, and know it by heart; or if they steal the **Apostle's** words and can repeat them:

<margin>Romans 1</margin>
**The Gospel is the power of God for salvation to everyone who believes.** <note>original: *Evangelium est potentia ad salutem omni credenti* (Romans 1:16)</note> As I also thought years ago, when I heard and learned it in the schools: But such knowledge is a knowing without sap or strength, without spirit and life.

# Of True Christianity #

# Christian Conversation #

<meta>catchword: through</meta>

through Christ the world is crucified to me and I to the world. He was in Christ and Christ in him, and he preached Christ crucified <note>Latin: "Christum Crucifixum"</note>, not as something alongside himself or from the outside <note>Latin: "ab extra"</note>, but within him; indeed, Christ spoke in him. When one preaches Christ as something alongside oneself from the outside, and does not preach oneself and all others as being crucified with Christ—so that one lives and walks in Him—then Christ crucified has not been preached. When one does not patiently suffer contempt, mockery, and disdain; when one does not wish to be crucified and put to death with Him, then it is impossible to preach Christ the crucified. Meanwhile, we sit in our official sermons and within the safety of the city walls, in warm rooms, and preach for wages to "mouth-Christians" <note>people who are Christian in name only</note>, and we do not go out from such people to those who have heard nothing of Christ. We should seek Christ's little sheep in the midst of wolves and not cease to drive them into the fold <note>original: "in orbem," literally "into the circle"</note> until death separates us by the hands of the godless—that would be preaching Christ crucified. Because we sit in comfortable security <note>original: "im gedinge," referring to a fixed or contracted position</note>, loving the satisfaction of our bodies, our wives, and our children more than the salvation of the sheep scattered among the wolves, we cannot preach Christ the crucified. 

Thirdly, they are ignorant of and deny the indwelling Christ <note>Latin: "ignorant & negant Christum inhabitantem"</note> or the essential union <note>Latin: "unionem essentialem"</note> of the believer with Christ. Almost all of them make do with "imputed righteousness" from the outside <note>Latin: "justitia imputativa ab extra," the theological concept that Christ's righteousness is legally credited to a person rather than internally changing them</note>. The world gladly accepts this and thanks God that Christ, from the outside, did everything for them in His own person. When someone speaks of the grafting or planting <note>Latin: "De insertione aut implantatione"</note> of the believer into Christ—of the essential <term>Einwohnung</term> <note>indwelling</note>—then they cry "Heretic!", "Osiander!" <note>Andreas Osiander, a reformer who taught that justification was an internal transformation</note>, "Fanatic!" <note>German: "Schwermer," a derogatory term for radical reformers</note>, etc. For it is not found at all in the <term>Augsburg Confession</term> <note>Latin: "Augustana confessione," the primary statement of faith for the Lutheran church</note>, to which so many cities, princes, and lands have sworn an oath.

<margin>
Romans 8.
1 Corinthians 13.
</margin>

Yet the union of Christ and the believer <note>Latin: "unio Christi vnnd Credentis"</note> must be physical <note>or "substantial"</note>—this and no other. For Christ justifies no one from the outside <note>Latin: "ab extra"</note>, but only from within <note>Latin: "ab intra"</note>. Indeed, His Spirit must be in us, otherwise we would not be His redeemed. So also must His flesh and blood be physically in us, so that we, united with His crucified body, [become] one seed...

<meta>This page continues the third point of a critique against clergy who deny the internal, physical reality of Christ's presence in the believer. It then transitions to a fourth point regarding the hypocrisy of their lives compared to their preaching.</meta>

81.

# of true Christianity. #

L

<meta>catchword: undestroy-</meta>

[...indestructi]ble will come to the resurrection, as all Scripture witnesses. But because they call such unshakable, healthy, and pure doctrine "heresy" <note>German: *Keßerey*</note> and reject the tabernacle of God among men <note>A reference to Revelation 21:3, emphasizing God dwelling within the human heart</note>, how can they preach Christ? How can they speak to another with fruitfulness and power of a thing which they themselves lack, and which they themselves cannot and will not suffer within themselves? The one who dwells in them is the one they preach; Satan dwells in them, so they can preach him as well, but not Christ crucified <note>Latin: *Christum crucifixum*</note>, no matter what they write or say.

Fourthly, they also cannot preach Christ crucified because their whole life is contrary to what they preach to others. Namely, they are all against the one they suppose themselves to be preaching. They think no differently; they believe they preach Christ crucified when they tell the story and explain it according to the "old man" <note>the unregenerate human nature or ego</note>—how Christ was crucified for the sins of the world, as if it were merely imputed to Him from the outside. They comfort themselves in His cross through a mere "mouth-faith" <note>German: *Maulglauben*; a faith of words and intellect that lacks inward transformation</note>, claiming that He has done enough, that He did it for us alone, and that we can do or merit nothing through our own following of Him, etc. 

Thus, all the preachers <note>Latin: *Predicanten*</note>, along with the universities and their listeners <note>Latin: *Auditoribus*</note>, remain in self-love, pride, self-interest, impurity, greed, anger, envy, usury, gluttony, injustice, etc. When one looks at their teaching or speech, it is only a human display and an elegant, haughty rhetoric; a powerless clanging, a tongue, a mouth, a piece of chatter. <margin>Psalm 20 <note>Likely referring to Psalm 120 in modern numbering, which mentions "the deceitful tongue" and "sharp arrows of the mighty."</note></margin> David calls such a thing a lying mouth, a deceitful tongue <note>Latin: *linguam dolosam*</note>, an arrow of the mighty, by which they lead land and people astray into ruin and damnation.

**M.** You have rightly uncovered the matter for him and have not minced your words <note>German: "taken no leaf before the mouth," an idiom for speaking bluntly</note>. If I were not so terrible and powerful against the Preacher <note>Latin: *Concionatorem*</note> that he must tremble before me, he would not tolerate you long on earth. But do not fear; you remain one with me, therefore I lead you to the one who sent me. But that other one I lead into the darkness, because he has not known Christ; for how should a proud man preach the humble Christ? How should a...

<summary>The author argues that preachers who lack the indwelling Christ actually preach the devil, reducing the Gospel to a "mouth-faith" that excuses their own immoral and prideful lives.</summary>
<keywords>mouth-faith, Christ crucified, clerical hypocrisy, indwelling Christ, spiritual fruit, deceitful tongue, old man</keywords>

...[un-died] <note>German: *vnerstorbener*; referring to someone who has not yet undergone the spiritual process of "dying to the self" or the ego</note> and un-mortified human proclaim the death of Christ? How shall one preach the patient, despised, and scorned Christ while striving after honor, titles, and names, and being unwilling to suffer anything? How will the wealthy glutton and drinking companion preach the poor, hungry Christ? One would have to preach the power of Baptism, Repentance, and the power of the Lord's Supper—the act of dying [to the self]—if one wanted to preach Christ crucified <note>Latin: *Christum crucifixum*</note>, and not merely dispute about the Sacraments, or shout at others as deceivers and desecrators of the sacraments. They are themselves what they accuse others of being: enemies of God and His Christ. And thus they nearly all die in such blindness, as I must daily experience and see throughout the entire world.

DEATH <note>Latin: *Mors*; here personified as a character confronting the dying man</note>. In body I am weak, but my soul comforts itself in the merit of Christ; there I hold fast to the suffering of Christ, which happened for me. BERNHARD. ### Of True Christianity.

from the heart of the one whom I have faithfully taught and preached; namely, Jesus Christ, His bitter suffering and death, and His blood-shedding which happened for me. I have indeed taught nothing else but the pure Gospel: how we all must be justified and saved through grace, not through our own merit, laid hold of through true faith. I ask you, therefore, to be a witness to this confession of mine in this world and the next—that I died in Christ, whom all sects and heretics <note>In this 17th-century context, "sects and heretics" likely refers to competing denominations or radical reformers who disagreed with the established Lutheran doctrine of the speaker.</note> persecute—and please grant me holy absolution and administer the holy Sacrament to me.

BERNHARD. Do you believe that my forgiveness is God’s forgiveness?

CONCIONATOR. Yes, as if God Himself stood there. For how else would we poor human beings become partakers of the forgiveness of sins, if God had not commanded and given such power to the minister <term>Absolution; the formal act of a minister pronouncing God's forgiveness to a repentant person</term>, and had not also immediately instituted the Sacrament for the forgiveness of sins, so that in the pangs of death we may draw the forgiveness of sins from it.

BERNHARD. Dear Lord and brother, may God preserve and confirm you in such true faith; and I, by the command of our LORD Jesus Christ, forgive you your sins, in the name of the Father + the Son + and the Holy + Spirit. Amen. Now receive the Sacrament of the Body and Blood of Christ, also for the forgiveness of sins.

CONCIONATOR. I thank you, LORD God, Heavenly Father, that you have given me Christ your Son as the Reconciler and Eradicater of my sins; Him whom I have faithfully taught and preached, and made known to many people through mouth and pen; Him whom all factions and heretics hate and persecute. Let me enjoy the benefit of this in the next world; I will firmly believe that you will not lie to me. For your Holy Scripture is true, which I have taught and explained in schools and churches. John in the third chapter says: <note>original: "Johannes am dritten Capittel sagt"</note> For God so loved the world that he gave his only begotten Son, so that all who believe in him should not be lost, but have eternal life.

# Christian Dialogue

Now I have indeed believed, and still firmly believe, that he was given into death for my benefit; <note>original: "Christus ist mein Leben/ Sterben ist mein Gewin"</note> Christ is my life, and dying is my gain; <note>original: "in deine Hende befehle ich dir meinen Geist"</note> into your hands I commend my spirit; you have redeemed me, you faithful God. I would like to turn toward the wall; please help me with your hands. Ah, now I lie comfortably; thus I will gently slumber.

**BERNHARDVS**. Do you wish to die in Jesus Christ, whom you have preached? Oh, see, he will not answer; perhaps he is already gone. Yes, he is gone quite softly and quietly, without any pain or spiritual struggle. May God help us all in such a way, that we may be able to make our confession and fall asleep through Christ in the presence of godly servants.

**Iohannes and Paulus**. You see, dear brother, a beautiful and glorious example in our dear, faithful <term>Seelsorger; literally "soul-carer," a traditional German term for a pastor or priest</term>, how he has passed away in a most Christian manner in the presence of the venerable gentlemen and <term>Prædicanten; preachers or ministers</term>. Therefore, we beg and exhorted you, that you would take heed of us and of yourself, and step away from the false, erroneous, and heretical teaching. Turn yourself to the healthy, pure doctrine of your dear pastor, so that you also—today or tomorrow—may die in peace and honor as a pious Christian. For if you were to persist in your false teaching, you would not only lose your good name, but you would also be the cause for us, your brothers, and your entire honorable lineage to be scorned, despised, and labeled as heretics. For what is more terrible to hear than what is said of you: that you are a <term>Schwermer; "enthusiast" or "fanatic," a derogatory term used by established reformers for those claiming direct divine inspiration</term>, a <term>Sacramentirer; "Sacramentarian," a label for those who denied the real physical presence of Christ in the bread and wine</term>, and a <term>Abtrünniger; an apostate or defector</term> from the pure doctrine?

**AVD.** Dear brothers, do not worry about these things; I cling to no human being on earth; alone in Christ Jesus do I desire to be found, until my end.

**PAV. IOHAN.** Yet others also cling to Christ along with your pastor; they do not let themselves be cried out as enthusiasts and heretics, as is happening with you. We tell you, we will hardly recognize or hold you as our brother; for your sake, our entire family is being dishonored. Who will visit you in your sickness? Who will hear your confession and grant you absolution?

# Of True Christianity
